## Files to read

- `crates/ql-oplog/src/op.rs` (Op enum, 13 variants)

## 5 verification tasks (in priority order)

### V1. Loro `LoroList::push` cross-peer merge semantic (HIGH priority — Opus M-1)

The design asserts (lines 99-100): "Loro's `LoroList::push` operations merge by Lamport timestamp" and "This **is** last-writer-wins on Lamport timestamps." Opus flagged this as unverified — could be causal-order-preserving only, not LWW.

**Read the Loro crate's `LoroList` source / docs.** Confirm or refute:
1. Two peers concurrently push to the same LoroList → both pushes preserved (yes/no).
2. The merged order is deterministic given the same set of operations regardless of peer-receive-order (yes/no).
3. If deterministic, by what ordering? Lamport timestamp? Peer-ID tiebreaker? Causal order with last-event-wins?

Output: cite the Loro source file + line number for the merge rule. If you can't find a definitive answer, say so explicitly.

### V2. `Op::BatchCommit` cross-peer contiguity (Opus M-5, Codex prior open question 3)

If peer A pushes a `BatchCommit { ops: [Op1, Op2, Op3] }` and peer B concurrently pushes `Op4` to the same LoroList, does the merged log preserve A's batch as a contiguous run, or could B's Op4 interleave between Op2 and Op3?

A's batch is ONE LoroList push (the BatchCommit IS one entry, with ops nested inside). So the answer should be "B's Op4 ends up before or after A's batch, never inside." Verify this by reading `ql-oplog::log::OpLog::append` (which serializes BatchCommit AS ONE JSON string and pushes ONCE) — confirm that BatchCommit really IS a single LoroList entry. Then confirm Loro's list merge can't split a single entry across peers.

### V3. Spill-anchor / spill-target ordering invariant under merge (Opus M-2)

Read `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/cells.rs::set_formula` + `::write_spill`. The producer-side workflow for `=SEQUENCE(3)` at A1:
1. `set_formula(A1, "SEQUENCE(3)")` emits `Op::PutFormula { A1, "SEQUENCE(3)" }`.
2. Internally, set_formula calls `eval_at_cell_boundary` which returns `EvalResult::Array(...)`.
3. `write_spill` clears any pre-existing spill at A1, then writes the spill body to A1:A3 (these are NOT separate ops; they're computed via recompute on replay).

Now under multi-peer merge: peer A logs `Op::PutFormula(A1, "SEQUENCE(3)")`; peer B concurrently logs `Op::PutValue(A2, 5)`. After merge:
- Causal order says: both ops applied in some order.
- If A's PutFormula is causally-before B's PutValue: replay sets A1's formula, spills to A1:A3 (overwriting A2=Blank). Then B's PutValue(A2, 5) lands on top.
- If B's PutValue is causally-before A's PutFormula: replay sets A2=5. Then A's PutFormula at A1 triggers spill; spill-block check at write_spill sees A2 has a value → emits #SPILL! at anchor A1.

**Verify**: is BOTH outcomes deterministic given the same causal order? Specifically, in case 2 (B before A), does the spill-block detection at write_spill rely on workbook state that is consistent across peers post-merge?

### V4. Format-id allocation under merge (Opus H-1)

Read `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/formats.rs::intern_format`. The producer-side logic:
```rust
let id = FormatId(self.workbook.formats().next_custom_id());
oplog.append(Op::RegisterFormat { id: id.0, string: s.to_owned() });
let allocated = self.workbook.formats_mut().intern(s);
debug_assert_eq!(allocated, id);
```

Under multi-peer merge: peer A and peer B both call `intern_format("X")` and `intern_format("Y")` concurrently. Both predict next-id N because both see the same workbook state at start. Both emit `Op::RegisterFormat { id: N, string: "X" }` and `Op::RegisterFormat { id: N, string: "Y" }`.

After merge: the LoroList has both ops. Replay's `FormatTable::register_at` (the same code path the producer used) rejects the second with `FormatRejectedSource::IdCollision`. Confirm this by reading `ql-storage::FormatTable::register_at`.

**The design's open question 5 proposes UUID-based ids.** Is there an alternative? Specifically:
- Could `next_custom_id` use a peer-ID prefix (e.g., (peer_id, counter) tuple) so concurrent peers never collide?
- What's the wire-format impact?

Output: yes/no on whether the IdCollision is a real bug + the cleanest resolution.

### V5. RenameSheet cross-peer bug (Opus H-2)

Scenario: peer A renames sheet "S" → "X" via `WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet`. The producer-side path (read `crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/sheets.rs::rename_sheet`):
1. Walks every stored formula via `iter_formulas`.
2. Rewrites cross-sheet refs via `rewrite_formula_text_for_sheet_rename`.
3. Emits a `BatchCommit` containing `Op::PutFormula(rewrite)` × N + `Op::RenameSheet { id, old, new }`.

Peer B concurrently writes a new formula `=S!A1+1` at cell B1 on sheet 0. Peer B's `Op::PutFormula { B1, "=S!A1+1" }` lands in the merged log AFTER A's BatchCommit (or before — depends on causal order).

**Verify**: in both causal orders, what does B see on its peer when it pulls the merge?
- A-before-B: B's formula text references "S" but the workbook now has "X". Replay binds → `BindError::UnresolvedName("S")` → `#NAME?` at B1.
- B-before-A: A's BatchCommit walks `iter_formulas` BEFORE B's op is in the workbook. So A's rewrite never sees B's "=S!A1+1". After merge, replay applies A's BatchCommit (rewriting cells A's peer saw), THEN B's PutFormula. Same result: B's formula at B1 references "S" not "X" → `#NAME?`.

**Confirm**: is this the actual behavior? Are there any other paths where the rename could correctly propagate to B's concurrent formula?

     9	---
    10	
    11	# Phase 5.1 — CRDT Data Model Decision
    12	
    13	## Purpose
    14	
    15	Lock the Loro container-shape decision for Phase 5 collaborative
    16	editing. The output is this design doc + a parallel Codex + Opus
    17	audit checkpoint. No code lands until both auditors sign off.
    18	
    19	## The question
    20	
    21	**How does Quantbook represent collaborative workbook state on
    22	top of Loro CRDTs so that 2+ peers can edit concurrently and the
    23	result merges deterministically?**
    24	
    25	The pre-existing single-writer `ql-oplog` already wraps a
    26	`LoroDoc`. The narrow question for 5.1 is whether to:
    27	
    28	- **(A) Keep the op-log shape.** Op-list-in-Loro is already a
    29	  CRDT under concurrent peer appends. Phase 5 adds transport +
    30	  snapshot + presence + undo without changing the state-on-Loro
    31	  representation.
    32	- **(B) Re-shape state as typed Loro containers per feature.**
    33	  Cells, names, sheets, tables, formats become LoroMap /
    34	  LoroList / LoroText containers directly. Merge semantics are
    35	  per-container-CRDT-native.
    36	- **(C) Hybrid — op-log for ordering-sensitive ops + typed
    37	  containers for grow-only sets.** E.g. cells stay in the op
    38	  log; sheets list becomes a LoroMovableList; names become a
    39	  LoroMap.
    40	
    41	## Recommendation
    42	
    43	**Adopt Option A (op-log preservation) as Phase 5 V1 with a
    44	narrow Option C extension for presence.**
    45	
    46	Rationale below. Option B is a Phase 5 V2 / Phase 6 candidate;
    47	the design doc explicitly defers it.
    48	
    49	## State of the existing `ql-oplog`
    50	
    51	What's there today (`crates/ql-oplog/src/log.rs`):
    52	
    53	```rust
    54	pub struct OpLog {
    55	    doc: LoroDoc,
    56	    cached_len: usize,
    57	}
    58	```
    59	
    60	- One `LoroDoc` per workbook.
    61	- All ops live in a single `LoroList` named `"ops"`.
    62	- Each op is JSON-serialized via `serde_json` and pushed as a
    63	  `LoroValue::String` into the list.
    64	- `OpLog::append` = serialize + push + commit.
    65	- `OpLog::iter` = list iterator → JSON-deserialize → `Op`.
    66	- Replay reconstructs `Workbook` state via
    67	  `ql_oplog::replay::replay_into`.
    68	
    69	The op vocabulary (13 variants in `Op` enum) covers every
    70	mutation the engine supports:
    71	- Cells: `PutValue`, `PutFormula`, `ClearFormula`
    72	- Names: `SetName` (workbook + sheet-scoped)
    73	- Sheets: `AddSheet`, `RenameSheet`
    74	- Formats: `RegisterFormat`, `SetCellFormat`
    75	- Tables: `CreateTable`, `DropTable`, `RenameTable`,
    76	  `RenameColumn`, `ResizeTable`
    77	- Workbook config: `SetReferenceMode`, `SetLocale`
    78	- Atomicity: `BatchCommit { ops: Vec<Op> }`
    79	
    80	## How Loro behavior changes when peers exchange updates
    81	
    82	Today's `OpLog` is single-writer (one runtime appends; replay is
    83	deterministic). The CRDT machinery is dormant. Phase 5 turns it
    84	on by:
    85	
    86	1. Each peer holds a `LoroDoc` representing the workbook.
    87	2. Peers exchange `LoroDoc` updates via Loro's
    88	   `export_from(version_vector)` + `import` API.
    89	3. Loro merges concurrent `LoroList::push` operations by
    90	   Lamport timestamp; concurrent pushes are PRESERVED, ordered
    91	   causally.
    92	
    93	The result: if peer A pushes `Op::PutValue { A1, 5 }` and peer B
    94	pushes `Op::PutValue { A1, 7 }` concurrently, the merged log
    95	contains BOTH ops in causal order. Deterministic replay then
    96	applies them in that order → final value = 7 (later in causal
    97	order).
    98	
    99	This **is** last-writer-wins on Lamport timestamps. It is the
   100	canonical CRDT-list semantic. We get it for free from Loro.
   101	
   102	## Why Option A is the right Phase 5 V1
   103	
   104	### Pro: reuses ~12 months of Phase 2-4 invariants
   105	
   106	Replay is a load-bearing path. Phase 4 closed dozens of edge
   107	cases (W5-156 dropped-table bind, W5-103 spill writeback, Tier
   108	C1 cycle detection, W5-91 rename-sheet formula rewrite, etc.).
   109	The replay path that Phase 5 inherits is the SAME path used by
   110	`.qbook` cold-load. Every Phase 4 fix carries forward.
   111	
   112	Container-shape (Option B) would require re-implementing those
   113	edge cases at the container level. We'd lose months of audit
   114	coverage.
   115	
   116	### Pro: 5.1 → 5.2 → 5.5 ships faster
   117	
   118	Per master plan estimates:
   119	- 5.1 (this doc): 3-5 days.
   120	- 5.2 (`ql-collab` core documents): 1-2 weeks under Option A;
   121	  3-4 weeks under Option B.
   122	- 5.5 (transport layer): same effort either way.
   123	
   124	Option A gets Phase 5 to a "two peers editing one workbook"
   125	demo in ~3-4 weeks. Option B is closer to 8-10 weeks for the
   126	same demo.
   127	
   128	### Pro: the JSON-string layer is opaque to merge semantics
   129	
   130	Loro's `LoroList` doesn't try to merge string contents. It
   131	treats each op as an opaque blob. Concurrent edits → both blobs
   132	preserved. This is EXACTLY the merge semantic we want for
   133	op-log-driven CRDT.
   134	
   135	If we used typed containers, we'd inherit Loro's
   136	per-container merge semantics — useful in some cases (LoroText
   137	for rich-text editing of formula text could enable
   138	character-level concurrent edits) and harmful in others
   139	(LoroMap merge for `name_table: LoroMap<String, NamedTarget>`
   140	would silently let two peers register the same name with
   141	different targets, picking one by LWW — but the engine's
   142	no-duplicate-name invariant might be violated mid-replay).
   143	
   144	### Pro: snapshots remain straightforward
   145	
   146	`LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Snapshot)` already produces a
   147	full-history blob. New peers import the snapshot and resume.
   148	Phase 5.5's offline-sync work uses `ExportMode::ShallowSnapshot`
   149	to compact old history. The mechanism is built-in.
   150	
   151	### Con: cell-level conflict diagnostics are coarse
   152	
   153	If peer A writes `=A1+1` to cell B1 and peer B writes `=A1*2`
   154	to cell B1 concurrently, the merged log has both ops. Replay
   155	applies them in causal order; the final formula is whichever
   156	op came last. We do NOT show the user "two concurrent edits;
   157	choose one" — we silently pick LWW.
   158	
   159	For Phase 5 V1 this is acceptable per master plan § 5.3
   160	(Conflict Resolution Semantics) which explicitly cites LWW as
   161	the V1 default. V2 work can add a "concurrent-edit detected"
   162	diagnostic by inspecting the Loro version vector at the LWW
   163	write site — but it's out of scope for 5.1.
   164	
   165	### Con: cell-level granularity is sub-optimal for very-large workbooks
   166	
   167	Every op carries a `sheet, row, col` address even when peers
   168	edit non-overlapping ranges. Loro merges the op list in O(n) on
   169	peer-count. For a workbook with 100k cells and 10 peers each
   170	making 1k edits, that's 10M list entries to merge. Performance
   171	testing post-5.2 will tell whether this is acceptable.
   172	
   173	Mitigation: Loro's `ShallowSnapshot` compaction (Phase 5.5)
   174	prunes old history. The op log is append-only-with-trim, not
   175	strictly append-only.
   176	
   177	## Recommended container shape
   178	
   179	### One `LoroDoc` per workbook
   180	
   181	Carry through from `ql-oplog`. Each workbook = one `LoroDoc`.
   182	Per-peer state lives in separate containers within the same doc
   183	(presence below).
   184	
   185	### Persistent state container: `"ops"` LoroList
   186	
   187	Unchanged from `ql-oplog`. JSON-string blobs of `Op` variants,
   188	appended in causal order, replayed deterministically.
   189	
   190	```
   191	LoroDoc
   192	└── LoroList "ops"
   193	    ├── 0: "{\"kind\":\"PutValue\",\"sheet\":0,...}"
   194	    ├── 1: "{\"kind\":\"PutFormula\",...}"
   195	    └── ...
   196	```
   197	
   198	### Presence container: `"presence"` LoroMap (per-peer ephemeral)
   199	
   200	New for Phase 5.6. Holds the per-peer cursor + selection +
   201	typing indicator. Keys are peer-IDs (UUIDs); values are JSON
   202	blobs with `{sheet, row, col, selection_end_row,
   203	selection_end_col, typing: bool}`.
   204	
   205	```
   206	LoroDoc
   207	├── LoroList "ops"
   208	└── LoroMap "presence"
   209	    ├── "peer-uuid-A": "{\"sheet\":0,\"row\":3,\"col\":2,...}"
   210	    └── "peer-uuid-B": "{\"sheet\":1,\"row\":0,\"col\":0,...}"
   211	```
   212	
   213	**Why MOM (Map of Maps) is wrong for cells but right for presence**:
   214	- Cells need ordering-aware merge (Tier C1 cycle detection
   215	  depends on op-log order; spill-anchor invariants depend on
   216	  set_formula-before-set_value-at-target ordering). Op-log
   217	  preserves this.
   218	- Presence is ephemeral and unordered — each peer overwrites
   219	  its own key. LoroMap LWW merge gives "last position update
   220	  wins per peer," which is exactly the desired semantic.
   221	
   222	Presence updates do NOT persist across `.qbook` save/load.
   223	Phase 5.6 wires the in-memory presence map; `.qbook` envelope
   224	v8 may or may not persist it depending on whether the use case
   225	demands "rejoin a session and see where teammates were" — out
   226	of 5.1 scope.
   227	
   228	### Snapshot container: `"snapshot"` LoroMap (reserved, V2)
   229	
   230	Phase 5 V2 may introduce a snapshot container that holds a
   231	compact representation of the workbook state at a recent
   232	version vector. Replay against a snapshot skips ops older than
   233	that vector. Reserved name; no Phase 5 V1 implementation.
   234	
   235	### Anti-pattern (do NOT do): one container per cell
   236	
   237	```
   238	LoroDoc
   239	└── LoroMap "cells"
   240	    ├── "0,0,0": LoroMap { value: ..., formula: ..., format_id: ... }
   241	    ├── "0,0,1": LoroMap { ... }
   242	    └── ...  // 100k+ inner containers
   243	```
   244	
   245	This is the textbook CRDT spreadsheet design (Google Wave, Y.js
   246	spreadsheet demos). It's wrong for Quantbook because:
   247	1. We lose op ordering. The spill-anchor / spill-target
   248	   invariant from Phase 4.7 depends on set_formula(anchor)
   249	   completing BEFORE set_value(target). Loro's per-container
   250	   merge doesn't preserve cross-container ordering.
   251	2. We lose the cycle-detection prerequisites. Tier C1 builds an
   252	   ephemeral calcgraph from the formula text; the formula text
   253	   needs to be coherent at every snapshot. Per-cell containers
   254	   could land mid-cycle.
   255	3. Loro overhead per container is non-trivial. 100k containers
   256	   for a 100k-cell workbook is the worst-case memory profile.
   257	4. Re-binding formula text after a sheet rename is op-log-aware
   258	   (W5-91 emits a `BatchCommit` containing PutFormula entries
   259	   for every rewritten cell + the RenameSheet op). Per-cell
   260	   containers can't express atomic batches.

   262	Option B / per-cell-container is Phase 5 V2 work at the

   266	
   267	## Conflict resolution semantics (Phase 5.3 preview)
   268	
   269	The 5.1 design picks Option A → conflict semantics are entirely
   270	LWW by Lamport-timestamp at the op-log level. Phase 5.3
   271	formalizes this with these defaults:
   272	
   273	| Concurrent op pair | Resolution |
   274	|---|---|
   275	| `PutValue { A1, v_a }` × `PutValue { A1, v_b }` | LWW |
   276	| `PutFormula { A1, f_a }` × `PutFormula { A1, f_b }` | LWW |
   277	| `PutValue { A1, v_a }` × `PutFormula { A1, f_b }` | LWW (op-log replay-order natural) |
   278	| `ClearFormula { A1 }` × `PutFormula { A1, f }` | LWW |
   279	| `SetName { foo, t_a }` × `SetName { foo, t_b }` | LWW |
   280	| `AddSheet "S"` × `AddSheet "S"` | First wins; second's replay errors via NameRejected (existing replay path). Phase 5.3 can add a "rename second to S(2)" auto-resolution. |
   281	| `RenameSheet { 0, A, B }` × concurrent edit on sheet 0 | Edit's formula text written under old name → after merge, formula references old name → bind fails → `BindError::UnknownTable` or unresolved sheet → emit `#NAME?` per design § 12.4. Phase 5.3 hardens this. |
   282	| `DropTable "T"` × concurrent edit referencing T | Edit appends with old table-ref → bind fails post-merge → `#NAME?`. |
   283	
   284	The 5.3 audit checkpoint validates these defaults against the
   285	Phase 4.12 megaudit's cycle-detection / spill / drop-table
   286	invariants — every Phase 4 edge case must survive the CRDT
   287	merge.
   288	
   289	## Undo/redo (Phase 5.4 preview)
   290	
   291	Loro 1.x exposes `LoroDoc::set_peer_id` + `UndoManager`. Each
   292	peer has its own undo stack tracking THEIR appends only.
   293	Undoing a peer's op removes that op from the log (semantically;
   294	implementation may use tombstones).
   295	
   296	This is local-undo, NOT collaborative-undo. Two users editing
   297	the same cell + one user undoing their edit produces a sane
   298	result: their edit is removed from the log; the other user's
   299	edit (if later in causal order) stays.
   300	
   301	Phase 5.4 wires this; 5.1 just confirms Loro provides the
   302	primitive.
   303	
   304	## Transport (Phase 5.5 preview)
   305	
   306	Loro's wire format:
   307	- `LoroDoc::export(ExportMode::Updates(version_vector))` →
   308	  bytes carrying only ops new-to-the-peer-since-version-vector.
   309	- `LoroDoc::import(bytes)` → merge into local doc.
   310	
   311	Phase 5.5 wraps these with a WebSocket transport (or any
   312	bidirectional byte channel). Each peer pushes version-vector
   313	updates to the server; server fan-outs to other peers; each
   314	peer imports.
   315	
   316	5.1 does NOT pick a transport (WS vs Server-Sent Events vs
   317	custom protocol) — that's 5.5's call. 5.1 just confirms the
   318	substrate works regardless.
   319	
   320	## Backwards compatibility with `.qbook` envelope
   321	
   322	The current persistence layer (`crates/ql-io`) saves the
   323	workbook as a `.qbook/` directory with JSON manifests +
   324	parquet cell data. Op log persistence (`ql-oplog::persistence`)
   325	saves the LoroDoc snapshot as `oplog.bin`.
   326	
   327	Phase 5 preserves both. The CRDT additions are purely additive:
   328	- `.qbook/oplog.bin` remains the same Loro snapshot blob.
   329	- Loading a `.qbook` still produces a single-writer-equivalent
   330	  workbook (no peer IDs, no presence).
   331	- Joining a session = load `.qbook` locally + receive Loro
   332	  updates from teammates + merge into the local doc.
   333	
   334	The pre-existing replay path handles the single-writer case;
   335	the new transport path handles the multi-peer case. Both call
   336	the same `replay_into` internally.
   337	
   338	## `ql-collab` crate scope
   339	
   340	Currently empty (`crates/ql-collab/src/lib.rs` is a 17-line
   341	placeholder).
   342	
   343	Phase 5.2 populates it with:
   344	
   345	```rust
   346	// crates/ql-collab/src/lib.rs
   347	
   348	pub mod session;     // CollabSession: per-peer state holder
   349	pub mod transport;   // trait Transport: send/recv update bytes
   350	pub mod presence;    // Presence map updates
   351	pub mod undo;        // peer-local undo via Loro UndoManager
   352	```
   353	
   354	Each module is small (~200-400 LOC). Total Phase 5.2 crate
   355	LOC estimate: ~1,500-2,500 LOC + tests.
   356	
   357	The crate depends on:
   358	- `ql-oplog` (for the LoroDoc + Op vocabulary)
   359	- `ql-storage` (to apply replay)
   360	- `ql-exec` (for `WorkbookRuntime` integration)
   361	
   362	It does NOT depend on `ql-io` or `ql-io-xlsx` — Phase 4.12
   363	Opus-C HIGH-3 (`ql-oplog → ql-io` cleanup, Tier D2 in v2
   364	backlog) is a separate pre-5.2 item. Phase 5.1 design assumes
   365	Tier D2 closes before 5.2.
   366	
   367	## Decision matrix summary
   368	
   369	| Dimension | Option A (op-log) | Option B (per-feature containers) | Option C (hybrid) |
   370	|---|---|---|---|
   371	| 5.2 effort | 1-2 weeks | 3-4 weeks | 2-3 weeks |
   372	| Phase 4 replay reuse | ✅ full | ❌ rewrite | partial |
   373	| Concurrent cell merge | LWW | per-cell CRDT | per-cell CRDT |
   374	| Cycle detection | works | needs re-design | partial |
   375	| Spill invariants | preserved | risk | risk |
   376	| Snapshot mechanism | `ExportMode::Snapshot` works | ditto | ditto |
   377	| Replay determinism | proven | needs new proof | partial |
   378	| Memory per workbook | low | high (100k containers) | medium |
   379	| Presence | new map | new map | new map |
   380	
   381	**Choose Option A** for Phase 5 V1. Revisit Option B/C as Phase
   382	5 V2 / Phase 6 candidate.
   383	
   384	## Audit checkpoint (gating 5.2)
   385	
   386	This design doc requires a parallel Codex + separate-Opus audit
   387	BEFORE any 5.2 code lands. Per the engine audit-discipline rule
   388	(`memory/quantbook_engine_audit_discipline.md`), the audit
   389	should:
   390	
   391	1. Independently re-derive the Option A vs B vs C tradeoff and
   392	   confirm or challenge the recommendation.
   393	2. Test the cycle-detection / spill-invariant / drop-table-rebind
   394	   claims against the actual Phase 4 code.
   395	3. Survey alternative CRDT data-model designs (Yjs, Automerge,
   396	   Diamond Types) and flag any patterns this doc misses.
   397	4. Probe the LWW conflict semantics for cases that produce
   398	   user-visible bugs (e.g., concurrent AddSheet "S" + edit on
   399	   sheet 0 by someone who hasn't seen the AddSheet yet).
   400	
   401	Audit transcripts will land at:
   402	- `docs/audits/2026-05-19-phase-5-1-codex.md`
   403	- `docs/audits/2026-05-19-phase-5-1-opus.md`
   404	- `docs/audits/2026-05-19-phase-5-1-consolidated.md`
   405	
   406	No 5.2 commits land until the consolidated audit doc is signed
   407	off.
   408	
   409	## Open questions for the audit checkpoint
   410	
   411	1. **Op-log compaction policy**: at what version-vector age do
   412	   we trim old ops? Master plan § 5.5 says
   413	   `ShallowSnapshot` is the answer; 5.1 design defers the
   414	   when-to-snapshot policy to 5.5.
   415	
   416	2. **Per-peer peer-IDs**: how are peer IDs assigned? Loro's
   417	   default is per-doc random `u64`; for collaboration we likely
   418	   want stable user-derived IDs. 5.2 picks.
   419	
   420	3. **`Op::BatchCommit` atomicity under merge**: today's replay
   421	   applies a batch's inner ops sequentially. With concurrent
   422	   peer appends, the inner ops are STILL contiguous in the
   423	   merged log (Loro preserves list-push contiguity per
   424	   peer). Verify this with Loro docs + a probe test.
   425	
   426	4. **Sheet-rename cross-peer correctness**: peer A renames
   427	   sheet "S" → "X" while peer B writes a cell with
   428	   `=S!A1+1`. After merge:
   429	   - If A's RenameSheet op is causally-before B's PutFormula:
   430	     B's formula text references "S" which no longer exists →
   431	     bind fails → `#NAME?`. Correct.
   432	   - If B's PutFormula is causally-before A's RenameSheet: A's
   433	     producer-side rewrite should catch B's text and emit a
   434	     PutFormula correcting it. But A doesn't see B's op until
   435	     merge → A's BatchCommit may not include the rewrite for
   436	     B's text. Bug? Phase 5.3 must address.
   437	
   438	5. **Format id collision**: two peers each call
   439	   `intern_format("X")` concurrently → both emit
   440	   `RegisterFormat { id: N, string: "X" }` with the SAME
   441	   predicted id. On merge, replay sees two register ops at the
   442	   same id with the same string → idempotent OK. But what
   443	   about `intern_format("X")` × `intern_format("Y")` predicting
   444	   id N? Two different strings at the same id → replay's
   445	   `FormatTable::register_at` rejects the second with
   446	   `FormatRejectedSource::IdCollision`. Phase 5.3 must
   447	   resolve.
   448	
   449	## Cross-references
   450	
   451	- `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` § Phase 5 (master spec).
   452	- `docs/phase5/entry-plan.md` (Phase 5 entry status).
   453	- `docs/PHASE-4-V2-BACKLOG.md` § D2 (`ql-oplog → ql-io`
   454	  cleanup; pre-5.2 prerequisite).
   455	- `crates/ql-oplog/src/log.rs` (the existing LoroDoc wrapper).
   456	- `crates/ql-oplog/src/op.rs` (the 13-variant `Op` enum that
   457	  Phase 5 inherits).
   458	- `crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs` (the deterministic replay
   459	  function that Phase 5's merge path reuses).
   460	- `memory/quantbook_engine_audit_discipline.md` (the 2-way
   461	  audit rule that gates this doc's sign-off).
   462	
   463	## Documentation deliverables (Phase 5.1 closure)
   464	
   465	- `docs/architecture/crdt-data-model.md` ✅ THIS DOC.
   466	- `docs/audits/2026-05-19-phase-5-1-codex.md` — pending.
   467	- `docs/audits/2026-05-19-phase-5-1-opus.md` — pending.
   468	- `docs/audits/2026-05-19-phase-5-1-consolidated.md` — pending.
   469	- `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` § Phase 5.1 update marking design
   470	  shipped + audit pending — pending.

     1	//! `OpLog` — Loro-backed append-only operation log.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase 2A.3.a (2026-05-12): scaffolding-only API. The log stores ops as
     4	//! JSON-encoded strings inside a single Loro `LoroList` container named
     5	//! `"ops"`. Each `OpLog::append` call serializes the `Op`, pushes a string
     6	//! into the list, and commits the Loro doc.
     7	//!
     8	//! ## Why JSON-in-Loro
     9	//!
    10	//! Per Round 7 architectural lock T1-D05, Loro is reserved for the op log
    11	//! layer. Phase 2A.3.a aims for minimum viable scaffolding — full CRDT
    12	//! semantics (LoroMap-per-op with typed fields) is reserved for Phase 5+
    13	//! when inter-peer merging becomes load-bearing. The JSON layer is
    14	//! invisible to callers: they see typed `Op` values via `iter`.
    15	//!
    16	//! ## Persistence
    17	//!
    18	//! `export_bytes` produces a `Vec<u8>` via `ExportMode::Snapshot`. The
    19	//! resulting blob includes the full state plus history; suitable for
    20	//! `oplog.bin` on-disk persistence (Phase 2A.3.c).
    21	//!
    22	//! `import_bytes` reconstructs an `OpLog` from such a blob. The reader
    23	//! probes the `"ops"` LoroList; if absent (or the wrong shape), the
    24	//! import returns `OpLogError::SchemaMismatch`.
    25	
    26	use loro::{ExportMode, LoroDoc, LoroList, LoroValue, ValueOrContainer};
    27	
    28	use crate::error::OpLogError;
    29	use crate::op::Op;
    30	
    31	/// The Loro container name our ops live under. Hard-coded; Phase 5+
    32	/// CRDT-level refactoring may add more containers (versions, peer state,
    33	/// etc.) but `"ops"` is reserved.
    34	const OPS_CONTAINER: &str = "ops";
    35	
    36	/// Append-only operation log backed by Loro.
    37	///
    38	/// Construct via `OpLog::new()` for a fresh log or `OpLog::import_bytes(...)`
    39	/// to reconstruct from a persisted snapshot. Mutations go through `append`;
    40	/// reads through `iter` / `len` / `is_empty`.
    41	pub struct OpLog {
    42	    doc: LoroDoc,
    43	    /// Cached count to avoid round-tripping into Loro for `len()`. Always
    44	    /// equal to `doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER).len()`; updated on append
    45	    /// and on import.
    46	    cached_len: usize,
    47	}
    48	
    49	impl std::fmt::Debug for OpLog {
    50	    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
    51	        f.debug_struct("OpLog")
    52	            .field("cached_len", &self.cached_len)
    53	            .field("doc", &"<LoroDoc>")
    54	            .finish()
    55	    }
    56	}
    57	
    58	impl Default for OpLog {
    59	    fn default() -> Self {
    60	        Self::new()
    61	    }
    62	}
    63	
    64	impl OpLog {
    65	    /// Construct an empty log.
    66	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    67	        Self {
    68	            doc: LoroDoc::new(),
    69	            cached_len: 0,
    70	        }
    71	    }
    72	
    73	    /// Append one `Op` to the log. Serializes via `serde_json`, stores as
    74	    /// a `LoroValue::String` in the `"ops"` LoroList, and commits the
    75	    /// underlying Loro doc.
    76	    pub fn append(&mut self, op: Op) -> Result<(), OpLogError> {
    77	        let json = serde_json::to_string(&op).map_err(OpLogError::Serialize)?;
    78	        let list: LoroList = self.doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER);
    79	        list.push(LoroValue::from(json.as_str()))?;
    80	        self.doc.commit();
    81	        self.cached_len += 1;
    82	        Ok(())
    83	    }
    84	
    85	    /// Iterate the log in append order. Each yielded item is a
    86	    /// `Result<Op, OpLogError>` — a deserialization failure yields
    87	    /// `Err(OpLogError::Deserialize { index, source })` and iteration
    88	    /// continues (no short-circuit). Callers wanting fail-fast can use
    89	    /// `iter().collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()`.
    90	    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Result<Op, OpLogError>> + '_ {
    91	        let list: LoroList = self.doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER);
    92	        let len = list.len();
    93	        (0..len).map(move |index| {
    94	            let entry = list.get(index).ok_or(OpLogError::SchemaMismatch(
    95	                "ops LoroList lost an entry between len() and get()",
    96	            ))?;
    97	            let value = match entry {
    98	                ValueOrContainer::Value(v) => v,
    99	                ValueOrContainer::Container(_) => {
   100	                    return Err(OpLogError::SchemaMismatch(
   101	                        "ops LoroList holds a container; expected JSON-string values",
   102	                    ));
   103	                }
   104	            };
   105	            let s = match value {
   106	                LoroValue::String(s) => s,
   107	                _ => {
   108	                    return Err(OpLogError::SchemaMismatch(
   109	                        "ops LoroList holds a non-string LoroValue; expected JSON-string",
   110	                    ));
   111	                }
   112	            };
   113	            serde_json::from_str::<Op>(&s)
   114	                .map_err(|source| OpLogError::Deserialize { index, source })
   115	        })
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    /// Number of ops in the log.
   119	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   120	        self.cached_len
   121	    }
   122	
   123	    /// True iff `len() == 0`.
   124	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   125	        self.cached_len == 0
   126	    }
   127	
   128	    /// Export the log to a binary blob suitable for on-disk persistence.
   129	    /// Uses Loro's `ExportMode::Snapshot` — includes full state + history,
   130	    /// compressed.
   131	    pub fn export_bytes(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, OpLogError> {
   132	        Ok(self.doc.export(ExportMode::Snapshot)?)
   133	    }
   134	
   135	    /// Reconstruct an `OpLog` from a previously-exported snapshot.
   136	    /// Probes the `"ops"` container to set the cached length; if the
   137	    /// snapshot doesn't carry our shape, returns `OpLogError::SchemaMismatch`.
   138	    pub fn import_bytes(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Self, OpLogError> {
   139	        let doc = LoroDoc::new();
   140	        doc.import(bytes)?;
   141	        let list: LoroList = doc.get_list(OPS_CONTAINER);
   142	        let cached_len = list.len();
   143	        Ok(Self { doc, cached_len })
   144	    }
   145	}
   146	
   147	#[cfg(test)]
   148	mod tests {
   149	    use super::*;
   150	    use ql_io::{CellWireValue, NamedTargetWire};
   151	
   152	    fn put_value(sheet: u16, row: u32, col: u32, n: f64) -> Op {

   154	            sheet,

   157	            value: CellWireValue::Number(n),
   158	        }

   160	
   161	    #[test]
   162	    fn new_log_is_empty() {
   163	        let log = OpLog::new();
   164	        assert!(log.is_empty());
   165	        assert_eq!(log.len(), 0);
   166	        assert!(log.iter().next().is_none());
   167	    }
   168	
   169	    #[test]
   170	    fn append_single_op_round_trips() {
   171	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   172	        let op = put_value(0, 5, 3, 42.0);
   173	        log.append(op.clone()).unwrap();
   174	        assert_eq!(log.len(), 1);
   175	        let read: Vec<Op> = log.iter().collect::<Result<_, _>>().unwrap();
   176	        assert_eq!(read, vec![op]);
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    #[test]
   180	    fn append_each_variant_round_trips() {
   181	        let ops = vec![
   182	            put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0),
   183	            Op::PutFormula {
   184	                sheet: 0,
   185	                row: 1,
   186	                col: 0,
   187	                text: "A1 * 2".to_owned(),

   189	            Op::ClearFormula {
   190	                sheet: 0,
   191	                row: 1,
   192	                col: 0,
   193	            },
   194	            Op::SetName {
   195	                scope: None,
   196	                name: "TaxRate".to_owned(),
   197	                target: NamedTargetWire::Constant {

   199	                },
   200	            },
   201	            Op::AddSheet {
   202	                name: "Inventory".to_owned(),

   205	            Op::BatchCommit {
   206	                ops: vec![
   207	                    put_value(0, 0, 0, 7.0),
   208	                    Op::PutFormula {
   209	                        sheet: 0,
   210	                        row: 0,
   211	                        col: 1,
   212	                        text: "A1 + 1".to_owned(),
   213	                    },
   214	                ],
   215	            },
   216	        ];
   217	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   218	        for op in &ops {
   219	            log.append(op.clone()).unwrap();
   220	        }
   221	        assert_eq!(log.len(), ops.len());
   222	        let read: Vec<Op> = log.iter().collect::<Result<_, _>>().unwrap();
   223	        assert_eq!(read, ops);
   224	    }
   225	
   226	    #[test]
   227	    fn binary_export_import_preserves_order() {
   228	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   229	        let ops = vec![
   230	            put_value(0, 0, 0, 1.0),
   231	            put_value(0, 0, 1, 2.0),
   232	            put_value(0, 0, 2, 3.0),
   233	            Op::PutFormula {
   234	                sheet: 0,
   235	                row: 1,
   236	                col: 0,
   237	                text: "A1 + B1 + C1".to_owned(),

   239	            Op::AddSheet {

   247	        let bytes = log.export_bytes().unwrap();
   248	        let restored = OpLog::import_bytes(&bytes).unwrap();
   249	        assert_eq!(restored.len(), ops.len());
   250	        let read: Vec<Op> = restored.iter().collect::<Result<_, _>>().unwrap();
   251	        assert_eq!(read, ops);
   252	    }
   253	
   254	    #[test]
   255	    fn import_empty_snapshot_yields_empty_log() {
   256	        let original = OpLog::new();
   257	        let bytes = original.export_bytes().unwrap();
   258	        let restored = OpLog::import_bytes(&bytes).unwrap();

     1	//! The `Op` enum — the engine's mutation vocabulary for the op log.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase 2A.3.a (2026-05-12): minimum producer set covering everything
     4	//! `WorkbookRuntime` and `WorkbookTransaction` can mutate through their
     5	//! public API. Future variants (sheet renames, sheet deletes, formatting
     6	//! changes) land alongside the API additions that produce them.
     7	//!
     8	//! Each variant mirrors a `Workbook` mutation method one-to-one:
     9	//!
    10	//! | `Op` variant       | Replays to                                       |
    11	//! |--------------------|--------------------------------------------------|
    12	//! | `PutValue`         | `Workbook::put_at`                               |
    13	//! | `PutFormula`       | `Workbook::put_formula`                          |
    14	//! | `ClearFormula`     | `Workbook::clear_formula`                        |
    15	//! | `SetName`          | `Workbook::set_name`                             |
    16	//! | `AddSheet`         | `Workbook::add_sheet_with_chunk_rows`            |
    17	//! | `RegisterFormat`   | `FormatTable::register_at` (W5-80)               |
    18	//! | `SetCellFormat`    | `CellFormatOverlay::set` / `::clear` (W5-80)     |
    19	//! | `BatchCommit`      | (recursive — applies each inner op in order)     |
    20	//!
    21	//! ## Wire format choices
    22	//!
    23	//! - `Arc<str>` is NOT used in wire fields. `serde` without the `rc`
    24	//!   feature can't deserialize `Arc<str>` directly. We store `String` on
    25	//!   the wire and convert at the replay boundary. Cleaner separation: the
    26	//!   wire format owns its strings; the runtime types share via Arc once
    27	//!   inside the workbook.
    28	//! - `ValueWire` and `NamedTargetWire` are reused from
    29	//!   `ql_io::qbook_format` (Phase 2A.8 shipped them). Sharing the
    30	//!   serialization vocabulary across the engine's three persistence
    31	//!   surfaces (qbook envelope, qbook JSONL, op log) prevents wire-format
    32	//!   drift.
    33	
    34	use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};
    35	
    36	use ql_io::{CellWireValue, NamedTargetWire};
    37	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    38	
    39	/// A mutation operation recordable in the op log.
    40	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
    41	#[serde(deny_unknown_fields, tag = "kind")]
    42	#[non_exhaustive]
    43	pub enum Op {
    44	    /// Single literal cell write. Mirrors `WorkbookRuntime::set_value`.
    45	    PutValue {
    46	        sheet: SheetId,
    47	        row: RowId,
    48	        col: ColId,

     1	//! Replay an `OpLog` against a `Workbook`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Phase 2A.3.a (2026-05-12): single entry point `replay_into(&OpLog, &mut
     4	//! Workbook, &FunctionRegistry) -> Result<usize, ReplayError>`. Returns the
     5	//! count of ops successfully applied; on failure, returns
     6	//! `ReplayError::At { index, kind }` carrying the position of the failing
     7	//! op and the underlying failure. The workbook is left in a partial state
     8	//! on failure (callers decide whether to discard, recover, or recompute).
     9	//!
    10	//! ## Replay semantics
    11	//!
    12	//! Each `Op` variant maps to one `Workbook` mutation:
    13	//!
    14	//! - `PutValue` → `Workbook::put_at` (after row/col bounds check).
    15	//! - `PutFormula` → `Workbook::put_formula` (text only; replay does NOT
    16	//!   re-evaluate — that's a separate step the caller drives via
    17	//!   `WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all`).
    18	//! - `ClearFormula` → `Workbook::clear_formula`.
    19	//! - `SetName` → `Workbook::set_name`. Reserved names (e.g. `AI`) surface
    20	//!   as `ReplayError::NameRejected`.
    21	//! - `AddSheet` → `Workbook::add_sheet_with_chunk_rows`.
    22	//! - `BatchCommit` → recurse on each inner op. Index reporting flattens:
    23	//!   a failure inside a BatchCommit reports the inner op's overall
    24	//!   position (current outer index + offset).
    25	//!
    26	//! ## Idempotence
    27	//!
    28	//! `replay_into` is idempotent only in the trivial sense: replaying twice
    29	//! against the same starting workbook produces the same final state IFF
    30	//! the underlying mutation methods are themselves idempotent (and they
    31	//! are — `put_at` / `put_formula` / `clear_formula` / `set_name` /
    32	//! `add_sheet` all are). Callers wanting at-most-once semantics across
    33	//! sessions track replay state themselves (Phase 5+ work).
    34	//!
    35	//! ## `registry` parameter
    36	//!
    37	//! Passed for API symmetry with `WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &registry)`.
    38	//! 2A.3.a's replay doesn't use it (formula evaluation is deferred to a
    39	//! separate `recompute_all` call), but keeping the parameter stable here
    40	//! avoids a breaking signature change in 2A.3.b/c when the eval path
    41	//! could land inside replay.
    42

   287	/// Replay every op in `log` against `workbook` in append order.
   288	///
   289	/// Returns the total count of ops applied on success. On failure, the
   290	/// workbook is in a partial state — `ReplayError::At { index, .. }` tells
   291	/// the caller how far replay got.

   297	    log: &OpLog,

   301	    let mut count = 0;
   302	    for (index, op_result) in log.iter().enumerate() {
   303	        let op = op_result.map_err(|e| ReplayError::Deserialize(index, e))?;
   304	        apply_op(&op, workbook, index)?;
   305	        count += 1;
   306	    }
   307	    Ok(count)
   308	}

     1	//! Cell mutation API for `WorkbookRuntime`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Tier D1 Step 3.7 (2026-05-18): extracted from `mod.rs` per
     4	//! `docs/architecture/workbook-runtime-split-design.md`. Six
     5	//! producer-side cell-mutation methods + 3 private spill helpers.
     6	//! Largest method-LOC submodule: `set_formula` alone is 417 LOC.
     7	//! Pure code move; no behavior change.
     8	//!
     9	//! Methods:
    10	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::set_formula`] — lex → parse → bind → eval →
    11	//!   put_at + put_formula. Phase 1 W5-10 + many follow-up
    12	//!   refinements (W5-103 spill writeback, W5-106 SEQUENCE,
    13	//!   W5-107 TRANSPOSE/FILTER, W5-147 canonicalization).
    14	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::set_value`] — literal write with op-log
    15	//!   emission and spill invalidation (W5-104).
    16	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::clear_formula`] — strip a cell's formula
    17	//!   association; Phase 2B.5 op-log producer with idempotency.
    18	//! - `write_spill` (private) — write the spill body for array-
    19	//!   returning formulas; runs `clear_spill_if_present` first to
    20	//!   dissolve any pre-existing spill at the anchor.
    21	//! - `write_anchor_error` (private) — write `#SPILL!` (or other
    22	//!   error sigil) at an anchor when spill cannot proceed.
    23	//! - `reextract_spill_footprint_readers` (private) — re-extract
    24	//!   calc-graph deps for every formula reading cells inside a
    25	//!   dissolved or transitioned spill footprint.
    26

    29	use ql_formula_syntax::{lex, lex_with, parse, print_with, FormulaSite};

    31	use ql_oplog::Op;

    42	impl<'a> WorkbookRuntime<'a> {

     1	//! Recompute pipeline for `WorkbookRuntime`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Tier D1 Step 3.6 (2026-05-18): extracted from `mod.rs` per
     4	//! `docs/architecture/workbook-runtime-split-design.md`. Five
     5	//! methods covering the formula-recompute surface (full pass +
     6	//! incremental + per-cell helpers). Pure code move; no behavior
     7	//! change.
     8	//!
     9	//! Methods:
    10	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all`] — HashMap-order full pass
    11	//!   with the Tier C1 cycle-detection pre-pass (2026-05-18,
    12	//!   commit 3451d90a03f). Used by `.qbook` load + replay.
    13	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::recompute_dirty`] — incremental graph-
    14	//!   driven recompute via Tarjan SCC over the attached
    15	//!   `CalcgraphSession`'s dirty set; cycled members get `#CIRC!`.
    16	//!   The performance path with session-attached callers (IDE).
    17	//! - `try_recompute_one_cached` (private) — lex+parse+bind+eval
    18	//!   one formula via the `PlanCache`.
    19	//! - `try_recompute_with_aggregate_cache` (private) — same path
    20	//!   with `AggregateCache` for `SUM(NamedRange)`-style cache
    21	//!   hits (Phase 3.6 AGG-3-01).
    22	//! - `try_recompute_with_simd_profile` (private) — recompute
    23	//!   with the SIMD-classification + spill-management orchestrator
    24	//!   used by `recompute_dirty`'s sorted-node loop.

    38	impl<'a> WorkbookRuntime<'a> {
    39	    /// Re-evaluate every formula in the workbook. Used after `load_workbook` to
    40	    /// refresh stale values (the qbook loader stores formula text + a sentinel
    41	    /// value; this method computes the real value).
    42	    ///
    43	    /// Iteration order is HashMap-arbitrary, so cross-cell dependencies may
    44	    /// evaluate in a non-deterministic order. Engine Phase 3 calcgraph
    45	    /// integration will add topological scheduling for deterministic + correct
    46	    /// dependency resolution (see `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` Phase 3.4; tracked as
    47	    /// GAP-R-01 in `docs/known-gaps.md`).
    48	    ///
    49	    /// Phase 2B.2 (2026-05-12): signature changed from `Result<usize,
    50	    /// RuntimeError>` to `RecomputeResult` (always returns; no Result
    51	    /// wrapper). The prior shape short-circuited on first failure and
    52	    /// dropped per-cell context; the new shape continues past failures
    53	    /// and aggregates them. See [`RecomputeResult`] for the contract.
    54	    ///
    55	    /// Cells that fail structurally (lex/parse/bind) keep their
    56	    /// pre-recompute values and formula text. Cells that succeed have
    57	    /// their value replaced. Cells whose evaluation produces an Excel-
    58	    /// canon error value (`#DIV/0!`, `#VALUE!`, etc.) are counted as
    59	    /// succeeded — those error values are normal cell contents per
    60	    /// Excel canon, not structural failures.
    61	    pub fn recompute_all(&mut self) -> RecomputeResult {
    62	        // **Tier C1 (2026-05-18 — Phase 4.12 Opus-B H-2 closure):**
    63	        // cycle detection. `recompute_all` is the no-session-attached
    64	        // fallback used by `.qbook` load + replay. Before this fix,
    65	        // cyclic formulas like `=A1+1` in A1 silently produced
    66	        // wrong values on each call (1, then 2, then 3, …) because
    67	        // HashMap-order evaluation just re-read the prior value.
    68	        //
    69	        // Strategy: build an ephemeral `CalcgraphSession` from the
    70	        // current workbook, mark every formula dirty, run Tarjan
    71	        // SCC via `schedule_dirty`, and write `#CIRC!` for every
    72	        // cell in a cycle BEFORE the HashMap-order eval loop runs.
    73	        // The acyclic remainder evaluates through the existing
    74	        // HashMap-order loop; any cell that reads from a cycled
    75	        // cell sees the freshly-written `#CIRC!` and propagates it
    76	        // via standard error semantics.

    79	        // time (the existing loop below also parses each via
    80	        // `try_recompute_one_cached`). Acceptable cost for the
    81	        // load/replay path; `recompute_dirty` is the performance
    82	        // path with session-attached callers.

    91	        //   cycled cell (`A1 = A1+1`) was overwritten, and B1 would

    93	        //

    95	        //   pre-existing spill anchor at the same address before

    98	        //   formula leaves stale spill target overlays in A2/A3.
    99	        //
   100	        // - *Opus H-1*: bind-failed formula nodes (those whose

   107	        //   parse/bind error surfaces via the normal `failures`

   109	        let cycled_cells: std::collections::HashSet<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = {
   110	            use crate::calcgraph_session::CalcgraphSession;
   111	            let mut session = CalcgraphSession::rebuild_from_workbook(self.workbook).session;
   112	            let formula_addrs: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId)> = self
   113	                .workbook
   114	                .iter_formulas()
   115	                .map(|(s, r, c, _)| (s, r, c))
   116	                .collect();

   118	                if let Some(node) = session.cell_node_for(*s, *r, *c) {
   119	                    session.mark_dirty(node);
   120	                }
   121	            }
   122	            let sched = session.schedule_dirty();

   124	                .cycled
   125	                .into_iter()
   126	                .filter_map(|n| session.cell_address_for(n))
   127	                .collect()
   128	        };
   129	
   130	        // **Codex H-1 / H-2 closure pre-pass:** write `#CIRC!` to
   131	        // every cycled cell BEFORE running the HashMap-order eval
   132	        // loop, and clear any spill anchor that previously lived at
   133	        // the same address. Order matters: writing `#CIRC!` before
   134	        // the loop guarantees a non-cycled dependent that reads
   135	        // from a cycled cell sees the error sigil and propagates
   136	        // it, instead of reading a stale prior value.
   137	        for &(sheet, row, col) in &cycled_cells {
   138	            self.workbook.clear_spill_if_present((sheet, row, col));
   139	            self.workbook
   140	                .put_computed_at(sheet, row, col, Value::Error(ErrorValue::Circ));
   141	        }
   142	
   143	        // Snapshot the formula list so we don't hold a borrow during eval.
   144	        let entries: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> = self
   145	            .workbook
   146	            .iter_formulas()
   147	            .map(|(s, r, c, f)| (s, r, c, Arc::clone(f)))
   148	            .collect();
   149	        let attempted = entries.len();
   150	        let mut succeeded = 0;
   151	        let mut failures: Vec<RecomputeFailure> = Vec::new();
   152	
   153	        for (sheet, row, col, formula_text) in entries {
   154	            // Tier C1: cycled cells were already written to `#CIRC!`
   155	            // in the pre-pass above and any stale spill cleared.

   161	            if cycled_cells.contains(&(sheet, row, col)) {

   163	            }

   166	                    // Phase 3.5 (CORR-25): formula outputs route to the

   171	                Err(error) => {

   184	                            sheet,
   185	                            row,
   186	                            col,
   187	                            Value::Error(ErrorValue::Name),
   188	                        );

   190	                    } else {
   191	                        failures.push(RecomputeFailure {
   192	                            sheet,
   193	                            row,
   194	                            col,
   195	                            formula_text,
   196	                            error,
   197	                        });
   198	                    }
   199	                }
   200	            }
   201	        }
   202	
   203	        RecomputeResult {
   204	            attempted,
   205	            succeeded,
   206	            failures,

   213	            // only. `recompute_all` is the legacy full-pass path.
   214	            simd_classified: 0,

   218	    /// **Engine Phase 3.4 (2026-05-12) — W5-37 SCH-3-01..04 entry
   219	    /// point.** Recompute only the formulas the attached

   221	    /// The scheduler runs iterative Tarjan SCC over the dirty

   226	    ///

   228	    /// runtime was constructed via `new` or `with_oplog`); the
   229	    /// dirty-set lives on the session, so we can't do incremental

   238	    pub fn recompute_dirty(&mut self) -> Option<RecomputeResult> {
   239	        // The session lives behind an `Option<&'a mut CalcgraphSession>`
   240	        // — take it out for the duration of this call so we can borrow

   246	        // **W5-106 (Phase 4.7.M / task #136 closure)**: fixed-point
   247	        // loop. Each iteration:
   248	        //   1. claim + schedule the current dirty set.
   249	        //   2. snapshot prior + originally_dirty for newly-seen nodes

   255	        //      on_set_value at spill-footprint cells, adding to dirty.

     1	//! Format API for `WorkbookRuntime`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Tier D1 Step 3.1 (2026-05-18): extracted from `mod.rs` per
     4	//! `docs/architecture/workbook-runtime-split-design.md`. Three
     5	//! methods covering the producer-side format surface — table
     6	//! interning, per-cell binding, and display rendering. Pure code
     7	//! move; no behavior change.
     8	//!
     9	//! Methods:
    10	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::intern_format`] — register a format string
    11	//!   in the workbook's `FormatTable` and emit `Op::RegisterFormat`
    12	//!   if (and only if) a new id was allocated. Idempotent.
    13	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::set_cell_format`] — bind a cell to a
    14	//!   `FormatId` (or clear via `None`). Refuses unknown ids
    15	//!   (`RuntimeError::UnknownFormatId`).
    16	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::read_display`] — read a cell and render it
    17	//!   through its `FormatId` binding (or General if unbound).
    18	//!   Memoizes parsed `FormatString` per id in
    19	//!   `WorkbookRuntime::format_cache`.
    20	
    21	use ql_functions::format;
    22	use ql_oplog::Op;
    23	use ql_storage::FormatId;
    24	use ql_types::{ColId, EvalContext, RowId, SheetId};
    25	
    26	use super::{validate_cell, RuntimeError, WorkbookRuntime};
    27	
    28	impl<'a> WorkbookRuntime<'a> {
    29	    /// **W5-82 (Phase 4.5.D part 6):** intern a number-format string into
    30	    /// the workbook's `FormatTable` and emit `Op::RegisterFormat` to the
    31	    /// op log if (and only if) the call allocated a NEW id. Returns the
    32	    /// `FormatId`, which the caller can pass to [`set_cell_format`].
    33	    ///
    34	    /// Idempotent on repeated calls with the same string — the second
    35	    /// call returns the same id without emitting a duplicate op. This
    36	    /// keeps the op log compact and replay deterministic.
    37	    ///
    38	    /// Direct callers of `Workbook::formats_mut().intern()` bypass the
    39	    /// op log silently; that path is documented as low-level and intended
    40	    /// for the qbook loader + tests + engine-internal reconstruction.
    41	    pub fn intern_format(&mut self, s: &str) -> Result<FormatId, RuntimeError> {
    42	        // Check whether the table already knows this string. If so, no
    43	        // new id is allocated and we MUST NOT emit `RegisterFormat`
    44	        // (idempotent path).
    45	        if let Some(existing) = self.workbook.formats().iter().find(|(_, t)| *t == s) {
    46	            return Ok(existing.0);
    47	        }
    48	        // New string — predict the id the table will allocate so we can
    49	        // emit the op BEFORE mutating (append-before-mutate ordering,
    50	        // matching set_name / set_value).
    51	        let id = FormatId(self.workbook.formats().next_custom_id());
    52	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
    53	            oplog.append(Op::RegisterFormat {
    54	                id: id.0,
    55	                string: s.to_owned(),
    56	            })?;
    57	        }
    58	        let allocated = self.workbook.formats_mut().intern(s);
    59	        debug_assert_eq!(
    60	            allocated, id,
    61	            "FormatTable::intern allocated a different id than next_custom_id predicted"
    62	        );
    63	        Ok(allocated)
    64	    }
    65	
    66	    /// **W5-82 (Phase 4.5.D part 6):** bind a cell to a `FormatId` (or
    67	    /// clear its binding by passing `None`). Emits `Op::SetCellFormat`
    68	    /// to the op log. Sheet/row/col are validated; `id = Some(...)` that
    69	    /// references an unknown id is REFUSED (matching replay's
    70	    /// `FormatNotRegistered` semantic — producer-side enforcement is
    71	    /// the cleaner spot for the check).
    72	    ///
    73	    /// Direct callers of `Sheet::format_overlay_mut()` bypass the op
    74	    /// log silently; that path is documented as low-level.
    75	    pub fn set_cell_format(
    76	        &mut self,
    77	        sheet: SheetId,
    78	        row: RowId,
    79	        col: ColId,
    80	        id: Option<FormatId>,
    81	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    82	        validate_cell(self.workbook, sheet, row, col)?;
    83	        // Producer-side gatekeeping: refuse `Some(id)` referencing an
    84	        // unregistered format. Replay does the same check (W5-80) so
    85	        // an op log that ever reaches the wire is well-formed.
    86	        if let Some(fid) = id {
    87	            if self.workbook.formats().lookup(fid).is_none() {
    88	                return Err(RuntimeError::UnknownFormatId(fid.0));
    89	            }
    90	        }
    91	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
    92	            oplog.append(Op::SetCellFormat {
    93	                sheet,
    94	                row,
    95	                col,
    96	                id: id.map(|f| f.0),
    97	            })?;
    98	        }
    99	        // Apply the mutation. After append-success this cannot fail.
   100	        let overlay = self
   101	            .workbook
   102	            .sheet_mut(sheet)
   103	            .expect("validate_cell guards sheet bounds")
   104	            .format_overlay_mut();
   105	        match id {
   106	            Some(fid) => {
   107	                overlay.set(row, col, fid);
   108	            }
   109	            None => {
   110	                overlay.clear(row, col);
   111	            }
   112	        }
   113	        Ok(())
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    /// **W5-82 (Phase 4.5.D part 6):** read a cell and render it through
   117	    /// its `FormatId` binding (or `General` if unbound). Returns the
   118	    /// final display string the IDE would show. The runtime's
   119	    /// `EvalContext` (date system, locale, now-provider) drives
   120	    /// date/time rendering.
   121	    ///
   122	    /// Lookup chain per call:
   123	    ///   1. cell's raw `Value` (cheap; lives on the storage hot path)
   124	    ///   2. format id from sheet's `CellFormatOverlay` (None → General)
   125	    ///   3. parsed `FormatString` from `format_cache` (parse + insert on miss)
   126	    ///   4. `format::render(&value, &fmt, &ctx)` → `String`
   127	    ///
   128	    /// If the cell's format references an id that's missing from the
   129	    /// `FormatTable` (corrupted state — should not happen in practice
   130	    /// because `set_cell_format` refuses unknown ids), falls back to
   131	    /// `General` rendering. If the format string fails to parse,
   132	    /// falls back to `General` and the renderer never errors.
   133	    ///
   134	    /// **W5-84 closure (Codex MEDIUM-2) cache-staleness caveat:** the
   135	    /// `format_cache` is keyed by `FormatId` and populated on first
   136	    /// `read_display` for that id. `FormatTable::register_at` rejects
   137	    /// id→string mutations, so cached entries stay correct for the
   138	    /// runtime's lifetime under the runtime's own append-only
   139	    /// `intern_format` path. HOWEVER, the low-level
   140	    /// `Workbook::formats_mut()` accessor (loader-only by convention but
   141	    /// `pub` for the qbook loader + tests) can register a new id AFTER
   142	    /// `read_display` has cached the General fallback for that id. In
   143	    /// that pathological sequence the cache will continue rendering
   144	    /// General until the runtime is rebuilt. Analogous to the
   145	    /// `WorkbookEnv` date-system caveat documented on
   146	    /// `Workbook::set_date_system`. Tracked as GAP-F-12 in
   147	    /// `docs/known-gaps.md`.
   148	    pub fn read_display(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> String {
   149	        let value = self.workbook.read(ql_types::Address::new(sheet, row, col));
   150	        let format_id = self
   151	            .workbook
   152	            .sheet(sheet)
   153	            .and_then(|s| s.format_overlay().get(row, col))
   154	            .unwrap_or(FormatId::GENERAL);
   155	        // Cache lookup. Vacant → resolve via FormatTable + parse + insert.
   156	        let needs_parse = !self.format_cache.contains_key(&format_id);
   157	        if needs_parse {
   158	            let parsed = self
   159	                .workbook
   160	                .formats()
   161	                .lookup(format_id)
   162	                .and_then(|s| format::parse(s).ok());
   163	            // Fall back to General if either lookup or parse failed.
   164	            let parsed = parsed.unwrap_or_else(|| {
   165	                format::parse("General").expect("'General' is a valid format string")
   166	            });
   167	            self.format_cache.insert(format_id, parsed);
   168	        }
   169	        let fmt = self
   170	            .format_cache
   171	            .get(&format_id)
   172	            .expect("just inserted above");
   173	        // V1 EvalContext: workbook's date_system + en-US locale + System now-provider.
   174	        let ctx = EvalContext {
   175	            date_system: self.workbook.date_system(),
   176	            ..EvalContext::default()
   177	        };
   178	        format::render(&value, fmt, &ctx)
   179	    }
   180	}
   181	
   182	#[cfg(test)]
   183	mod tests {
   184	    use ql_functions::default_registry;
   185	    use ql_oplog::{Op, OpLog};
   186	    use ql_storage::{FormatId, Workbook};
   187	    use ql_types::{ErrorValue, Value};
   188	
   189	    use crate::workbook_runtime::{RuntimeError, WorkbookRuntime};
   190	
   191	    // ===== W5-82 / Phase 4.5.D part 6 — format runtime wrappers =====
   192	
   193	    #[test]
   194	    fn intern_format_returns_existing_builtin_id_no_oplog_op() {
   195	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
   196	        let reg = default_registry();
   197	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   198	        {
   199	            let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_oplog(&mut wb, &reg, &mut log);
   200	            // "General" is built-in id 0; pre-populated by FormatTable::default().
   201	            let id = rt.intern_format("General").unwrap();
   202	            assert_eq!(id, FormatId::GENERAL);
   203	            let id2 = rt.intern_format("0.00").unwrap();
   204	            assert_eq!(id2, FormatId(2));
   205	        }
   206	        // No RegisterFormat ops should have been emitted — both strings
   207	        // are pre-populated built-ins.
   208	        let ops: Vec<_> = log.iter().collect();
   209	        assert_eq!(ops.len(), 0, "built-ins must not emit RegisterFormat ops");
   210	    }
   211	
   212	    #[test]
   213	    fn intern_format_new_string_emits_register_op() {
   214	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
   215	        let reg = default_registry();
   216	        let mut log = OpLog::new();
   217	        {
   218	            let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_oplog(&mut wb, &reg, &mut log);
   219	            let id = rt.intern_format("\"€\" #,##0.00").unwrap();
   220	            assert_eq!(id.0, ql_storage::FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID);

     1	//! Sheet mutation API for `WorkbookRuntime`.
     2	//!
     3	//! Tier D1 Step 3.3 (2026-05-18): extracted from `mod.rs` per
     4	//! `docs/architecture/workbook-runtime-split-design.md`. Two
     5	//! producer-side sheet methods + a tokenization-aware formula-text
     6	//! rewriter helper used by rename_sheet. Pure code move; no
     7	//! behavior change.
     8	//!
     9	//! Methods:
    10	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::add_sheet`] (W5-92) — append a new sheet,
    11	//!   validate name + chunk_rows, emit `Op::AddSheet`.
    12	//! - [`WorkbookRuntime::rename_sheet`] (W5-91) — rename by id,
    13	//!   rewrite cross-sheet refs in stored formula text via
    14	//!   lex→parse→rewrite→print, emit `BatchCommit` containing every
    15	//!   `PutFormula` rewrite + the `RenameSheet` op.
    16	
    17	use std::sync::Arc;
    18	
    19	use ql_oplog::Op;
    20	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId, SheetId};
    21	
    22	use super::{RuntimeError, WorkbookRuntime};

    24	/// **W5-91 (Phase 4.6.C):** rewrite cross-sheet refs in a formula text
    25	/// when a sheet is renamed. Round-trips `text → lex → parse → rewrite
    26	/// → print` so substitution is tokenization-aware (string literals,
    27	/// quoted-sheet escapes, function calls, etc. all get the right
    28	/// treatment).
    29	///
    30	/// Returns `Some(new_text)` if the rewrite changed the formula,
    31	/// `None` otherwise (so the caller can skip the storage write +
    32	/// op-log entry when there's nothing to record).
    33	///
    34	/// Defensive: if `lex` or `parse` fails (corrupted historical text),
    35	/// returns `None` rather than propagating an error — the rename
    36	/// shouldn't break otherwise-recoverable workbooks.
    37	fn rewrite_formula_text_for_sheet_rename(
    38	    text: &str,
    39	    old_canonical: &str,
    40	    new_name: &Arc<str>,
    41	) -> Option<String> {
    42	    let stripped = text.strip_prefix('=').unwrap_or(text);
    43	    let tokens = ql_formula_syntax::lex(stripped).ok()?;
    44	    let expr = ql_formula_syntax::parse(tokens).ok()?;
    45	    let rewritten = ql_formula_syntax::rewrite_sheet_name_in_expr(&expr, old_canonical, new_name);
    46	    if rewritten == expr {
    47	        // Nothing changed; preserve the original text (avoids spurious
    48	        // op-log entries and skips the round-trip-printing's canonical-
    49	        // form rewrites for formulas that have no sheet references).
    50	        return None;
    51	    }
    52	    let printed = ql_formula_syntax::print(&rewritten);
    53	    // Preserve a leading `=` if the original had one.
    54	    let with_eq = if text.starts_with('=') {
    55	        format!("={printed}")
    56	    } else {
    57	        printed
    58	    };
    59	    Some(with_eq)
    60	}

    66	    ///

    68	    /// default) unless you have a specific reason — the qbook loader uses
    69	    /// the saved chunk_rows so layouts round-trip.

    71	    /// Direct callers of `Workbook::add_sheet` / `add_sheet_with_chunk_rows`
    72	    /// bypass the op log silently — that path is documented as low-level.

    80	        // so a bad value can't leave a phantom AddSheet in the log followed
    81	        // by a process-killing panic from `ColumnStore::with_chunk_rows` or
    82	        // `Workbook::add_sheet_with_chunk_rows` (assert on `id >= SheetId::MAX`).

    85	        }

    98	        // mutation. Codex HIGH-1 flagged that the prior path silently
    99	        // accepted any name at the storage boundary.

   147	        &mut self,
   148	        id: SheetId,
   149	        new_name: impl Into<String>,
   150	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   151	        let new_name: String = new_name.into();
   152	        // Capture old name BEFORE any mutation (defensive — if anything
   153	        // below errors, the workbook stays as-is).
   154	        let old_name = self.workbook.sheet(id).map(|s| s.name().to_owned()).ok_or(
   155	            RuntimeError::InvalidSheet {
   156	                sheet: id,
   157	                sheet_count: self.workbook.sheet_count(),
   158	            },
   159	        )?;
   160	        // Validate new name. Skip the validation if canonical name is
   161	        // unchanged (case-only rename is allowed); `Workbook::rename_sheet`
   162	        // will apply the display update.
   163	        let old_canonical = ql_storage::Workbook::canonical_sheet_name(&old_name);
   164	        let new_canonical = ql_storage::Workbook::canonical_sheet_name(&new_name);
   165	        if new_canonical != old_canonical {
   166	            self.workbook.validate_sheet_name(&new_name)?;
   167	        }
   168	        // Rewrite stored formula text. Collect rewrites into a batch so
   169	        // the op log records them alongside the rename atomically.
   170	        let new_name_arc: Arc<str> = Arc::from(new_name.as_str());
   171	        let mut rewritten_cells: Vec<(SheetId, RowId, ColId, String)> = Vec::new();
   172	        for (sheet, row, col, text) in self.workbook.iter_formulas() {
   173	            if let Some(new_text) =
   174	                rewrite_formula_text_for_sheet_rename(text.as_ref(), &old_canonical, &new_name_arc)
   175	            {
   176	                rewritten_cells.push((sheet, row, col, new_text));
   177	            }
   178	        }
   179	        // (Phase 4.6.D / future): walk NamedTarget::Formula entries for
   180	        // the same rewrite. Today named formulas are deferred so
   181	        // formula_cells is the only text-bearing surface to rewrite.
   182	
   183	        // Append-before-mutate: emit the BatchCommit op first so a
   184	        // failing append leaves storage unchanged. The batch carries:
   185	        //   [PutFormula(cell, new_text)] × N  +  RenameSheet
   186	        if let Some(oplog) = self.oplog.as_deref_mut() {
   187	            let mut ops: Vec<Op> = Vec::with_capacity(rewritten_cells.len() + 1);
   188	            for (s, r, c, ref new_text) in &rewritten_cells {
   189	                ops.push(Op::PutFormula {
   190	                    sheet: *s,
   191	                    row: *r,
   192	                    col: *c,
   193	                    text: new_text.clone(),
   194	                });
   195	            }
   196	            ops.push(Op::RenameSheet {
   197	                id,
   198	                old_name: old_name.clone(),
   199	                new_name: new_name.clone(),
   200	            });
   201	            oplog.append(Op::BatchCommit { ops })?;
   202	        }
   203	        // Apply mutations. Rewrites go in first so the storage layer
   204	        // never sees a state where the sheet name is new but formula
   205	        // text still references the old name. (Order matters only for
   206	        // observers that race — single-threaded mutation doesn't care,
   207	        // but the invariant is cleaner.)
   208	        for (s, r, c, new_text) in rewritten_cells {
   209	            self.workbook.put_formula(s, r, c, new_text.as_str());
   210	        }
   211	        // Swap the name. Cannot fail here — validation already passed.
   212	        self.workbook
   213	            .rename_sheet(id, new_name)
   214	            .expect("rename_sheet validation already passed");
   215	        // Wipe the PlanCache so cached bound plans (which baked in the
   216	        // old SheetId via SheetRef::Name resolution) miss on next
   217	        // recompute and rebind against the new sheet name. Sheet
   218	        // rename is edit-rate, not recompute-rate; a full cache flush
   219	        // is acceptable per design § 10.5 / Codex MEDIUM-7. (A
   220	        // finer-grained `sheet_gen` counter could be wired in a follow-
   221	        // up — filed as a future polish item.)
   222	        self.plan_cache.clear();
   223	        Ok(())
   224	    }
   225	}

   636	
   637	    /// Phase 2B.3 helper for `recompute_all`: consult the bind-plan cache
   638	    /// before doing lex/parse/bind work, then evaluate. Failures (lex /
   639	    /// parse / bind) propagate as `RuntimeError`; the caller bundles them
   640	    /// into a `RecomputeFailure`. Successful binds are cached so a
   641	    /// subsequent recompute (or a `set_formula` editing a nearby cell
   642	    /// with the same text) hits.
   643	    ///
   644	    /// Phase 3.6 (W5-39): this is the no-aggregate-cache wrapper. The
   645	    /// `recompute_dirty` path uses
   646	    /// [`Self::try_recompute_with_aggregate_cache`] which threads the
   647	    /// session's aggregate cache through the evaluator.
   648	    fn try_recompute_one_cached(
   649	        &mut self,
   650	        sheet: SheetId,
   651	        row: RowId,
   652	        col: ColId,
   653	        formula_text: &Arc<str>,
   654	    ) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
   655	        self.try_recompute_with_aggregate_cache(
   656	            sheet,
   657	            row,
   658	            col,
   659	            formula_text,
   660	            &crate::aggregate_cache::NoAggregateCache,
   661	        )
   662	    }
   663	
   664	    /// Phase 3.6 (W5-39): variant of `try_recompute_one_cached` that
   665	    /// threads an `AggregateCache` through the scalar evaluator so
   666	    /// `SUM(Sales)` / `AVERAGE(Sales)` calls consult + populate the
   667	    /// cache. Used by `recompute_dirty` which owns a session-side
   668	    /// `InMemAggregateCache`.
   669	    ///
   670	    /// **W5-103 (#128):** also takes `row, col` so the spill-writeback
   671	    /// branch inside `try_recompute_with_simd_profile` can materialize
   672	    /// arrays at the correct anchor cell.
   673	    fn try_recompute_with_aggregate_cache(
   674	        &mut self,
   675	        sheet: SheetId,
   676	        row: RowId,
   677	        col: ColId,
   678	        formula_text: &Arc<str>,
   679	        agg_cache: &dyn crate::aggregate_cache::AggregateCache,
   680	    ) -> Result<Value, RuntimeError> {
   681	        self.try_recompute_with_simd_profile(sheet, row, col, formula_text, agg_cache)
   682	            .map(|(v, _, _, _)| v)
   683	    }
   684	
   685	    /// Phase 3.9 (W5-42): like `try_recompute_with_aggregate_cache`
   686	    /// but ALSO returns a `bool` for whether the formula's bound plan
   687	    /// was SIMD-eligible per `crate::lower::classify`. The bool is
   688	    /// pure observability — the actual SIMD dispatch via `simd::*`
   689	    /// happens at the bench / FormulaRegion path. `recompute_dirty`
   690	    /// uses this to populate `RecomputeResult.simd_classified` so the
   691	    /// IDE profile can show where region optimization would help.
   692	    fn try_recompute_with_simd_profile(
   693	        &mut self,
   694	        sheet: SheetId,
   695	        row: RowId,
   696	        col: ColId,
   697	        formula_text: &Arc<str>,
   698	        agg_cache: &dyn crate::aggregate_cache::AggregateCache,
   699	    ) -> Result<(Value, bool, Option<SpillShape>, Option<SpillShape>), RuntimeError> {
   700	        let name_gen = self.workbook.names().generation();
   701	        // **W5-150 (Phase 4.9.O HIGH-1):** cell-aware key when `@`
   702	        // is present in the stored formula text. This is the
   703	        // recompute path — same canonical text at different cells
   704	        // would otherwise share the cell-specific `@`-narrowed
   705	        // plan from the first bind, leaking the wrong cell ref.
   706	        let cell_anchor = if formula_text.contains('@') {
   707	            Some((row, col))
   708	        } else {
   709	            None
   710	        };
   711	        let cache_key = PlanCacheKey {
   712	            text: Arc::clone(formula_text),
   713	            sheet,
   714	            name_gen,
   715	            cell_anchor,
   716	        };
   717	        // Borrow split: we need an immutable view of the workbook
   718	        // (for `names()` inside the closure) while holding a mutable
   719	        // borrow on `self.plan_cache`. Re-borrow the workbook reference
   720	        // by name so Rust's borrow checker can split them — both fields
   721	        // are disjoint subfields of `self`.
   722	        let workbook: &Workbook = self.workbook;
   723	        let plan: Arc<crate::plan::ExprPlan> =
   724	            self.plan_cache
   725	                .get_or_insert::<_, RuntimeError>(cache_key, || {
   726	                    // **W5-147 (Phase 4.9.K):** formula_text here is the
   727	                    // CANONICAL (A1+EnUs) text stored at set_formula
   728	                    // time. Lex with the canonical mode/locale —
   729	                    // regardless of the workbook's current
   730	                    // reference_mode + locale settings, the stored text
   731	                    // is always A1+EnUs per design § 4.4.
   732	                    let tokens = lex_with(
   733	                        formula_text.as_ref(),
   734	                        ql_types::ReferenceMode::A1,
   735	                        ql_types::Locale::EnUs,
   736	                    )?;
   737	                    let expr = parse(tokens)?;
   738	                    // W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D): pass `workbook` for names so
   739	                    // the two-tier sheet-then-workbook scope chain fires.
   740	                    // **W5-114 (Phase 4.8.E):** carry recomputed cell address.
   741	                    Ok(bind_with_site(
   742	                        &expr,
   743	                        BindSite::at_cell(ql_types::Address::new(sheet, row, col)),
   744	                        workbook,
   745	                        workbook,
   746	                        workbook,
   747	                    )?)
   748	                })?;
