   750	        // Phase 3.9: classify the plan against the SIMD kernel set.
   751	        // Pure function over the plan tree; no allocation.
   752	        let simd_eligible = crate::lower::classify(plan.as_ref()).is_applicable();
   753	
   754	        // **W5-103 megaudit HIGH-2 closure (#128, all 3 reviewers
   755	        // cross-confirmed):** route through `eval_at_cell_boundary`
   756	        // (same entry set_formula uses) so a top-level
   757	        // `ExprPlan::Array` produces an `EvalResult::Array` here and
   758	        // materializes via `write_spill` — instead of collapsing to
   759	        // `#CALC!` (which `eval_scalar_with_cache` returns per the
   760	        // scalar-context contract at scalar.rs:95).
   761	        //
   762	        // Without this, op-log replay of `PutFormula { text: "{1,2,3}" }`
   763	        // followed by `recompute_all` would write #CALC! at the
   764	        // anchor instead of spilling — design § 7.3/§ 12.3
   765	        // "load → recompute re-derives spills" contract broken,
   766	        // persistence layer (4.7.L) hard-blocked.
   767	        //
   768	        // The clear-old + write_spill sequence mirrors set_formula's
   769	        // 4.7.J.2 pattern. Unlike set_formula, we DO NOT fire
   770	        // on_set_formula / on_set_value / re-extract hooks: recompute
   771	        // doesn't change a formula's identity, only its computed
   772	        // value. Downstream readers were already dirtied by whatever
   773	        // made THIS formula dirty in the first place.
   774	        //
   775	        // **W5-108 (Phase 4.7.O) — Codex MEDIUM-5 closure**: clear
   776	        // the OLD spill footprint BEFORE eval, matching design § 10.3
   777	        // step 4 ("clear-old-before-eval") and `set_formula`'s
   778	        // identical ordering at line 621. Pre-fix ordering was
   779	        // eval → clear → write, which let a formula's eval observe
   780	        // its own OLD spill-target computed overlays through indirect
   781	        // reads (named ranges, aggregates). Direct self-reads create
   782	        // a producer-alias self-loop (Tarjan → #CIRC!) so the gap was
   783	        // narrow, but the design contract is "clear-before-eval" and
   784	        // bringing the code into alignment removes a latent
   785	        // composition footgun.
   786	        //
   787	        // We capture old_spill_shape BEFORE clear so the caller
   788	        // (recompute_dirty's fixed-point loop) can fire hooks for the
   789	        // dissolved footprint after eval+write_spill complete.
   790	        let old_spill_shape = self.workbook.spill_anchor_at(sheet, row, col).copied();
   791	        self.workbook.clear_spill_if_present((sheet, row, col));
   792	
   793	        let result: crate::eval_result::EvalResult = {
   794	            // **W5-117 (Phase 4.8.G.2):** carry recomputed cell for
   795	            // structured-ref `[@Col]` row narrowing.
   796	            let env = WorkbookEnv::with_formula_cell(
   797	                self.workbook,
   798	                ql_types::Address::new(sheet, row, col),
   799	            );
   800	            crate::scalar::eval_at_cell_boundary(plan.as_ref(), &env, self.registry, agg_cache)
   801	        };
   802	
   803	        // **Note (Sonnet #128 LOW-1, deferred):** the Array arm here
   804	        // writes the anchor via `write_spill`; the caller also
   805	        // `put_computed_at(anchor, anchor_value)` — a no-op duplicate
   806	        // write. Deferred; harmless.
   807	        //
   808	        // **W5-106 (Phase 4.7.M / task #136 closure)**: capture the
   809	        // NEW shape via write_spill's return. We DON'T fire
   810	        // on_set_value hooks here — the caller (recompute_dirty in
   811	        // particular) owns the session reference at this scope
   812	        // (recompute_dirty `take()`s `self.graph` into a local, so
   813	        // `self.graph` is `None` during the sorted loop). Bubble the
   814	        // shape info up via the return; the caller fires hooks
   815	        // against its session.
   816	        let (anchor_value, new_spill_shape) = match result {
   817	            crate::eval_result::EvalResult::Scalar(v) => (v, None),
   818	            crate::eval_result::EvalResult::Array(array) => {
   819	                let formula_text_arc = self
   820	                    .workbook
   821	                    .formula_at(sheet, row, col)
   822	                    .cloned()
   823	                    .expect("recompute: formula_at MUST be Some — we're recomputing this cell");
   824	                self.write_spill(sheet, row, col, array, formula_text_arc)
   825	            }
   826	        };
   827	
   828	        Ok((
   829	            anchor_value,
   830	            simd_eligible,
   831	            old_spill_shape,
   832	            new_spill_shape,
   833	        ))
   834	    }
   835	}

   837	#[cfg(test)]
   838	mod tests {
   839	    use ql_functions::default_registry;
   840	    use ql_storage::Workbook;
   841	    use ql_types::{ErrorValue, Value};
   842	
   843	    use crate::plan::BindError;
   844	    use crate::workbook_runtime::{RuntimeError, WorkbookRuntime};
   845	
   846	    fn make_runtime_workbook() -> Workbook {
   847	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();
   848	        wb.add_sheet("S");
   849	        wb
   850	    }
   851	
   852	    // ===== recompute_all =====
   853	
   854	    #[test]
   855	    fn recompute_all_on_empty_workbook_is_noop() {
   856	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
   857	        let reg = default_registry();
   858	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
   859	
   860	        let result = rt.recompute_all();
   861	        assert_eq!(result.attempted, 0);
   862	        assert_eq!(result.succeeded, 0);
   863	        assert!(result.is_complete());
   864	    }
   865	
   866	    #[test]
   867	    fn recompute_all_refreshes_formula_values() {
   868	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
   869	
   870	        // Set up scenario: A1 = 5; B1 has formula =A1 * 2 evaluated as 10.
   871	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(5.0));
   872	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 1, Value::Number(10.0));
   873	        wb.put_formula(0, 0, 1, "A1 * 2");
   874	
   875	        // Change A1 to 100 (simulating a user edit that didn't auto-recompute).
   876	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(100.0));
   877	        // B1 still shows 10 (stale).
   878	        assert_eq!(
   879	            wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 1)),
   880	            Value::Number(10.0)
   881	        );
   882	
   883	        let reg = default_registry();
   884	        let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &reg);
   885	        let result = rt.recompute_all();
   886	        assert_eq!(result.attempted, 1);
   887	        assert_eq!(result.succeeded, 1);
   888	        assert!(result.is_complete());
   889	
   890	        // B1 now shows 200 (refreshed).
   891	        assert_eq!(
   892	            wb.read(ql_types::Address::new(0, 0, 1)),
   893	            Value::Number(200.0)
   894	        );
   895	    }
   896	
   897	    #[test]
   898	    fn recompute_all_handles_multiple_formulas() {
   899	        let mut wb = make_runtime_workbook();
   900	        wb.put_at(0, 0, 0, Value::Number(2.0));
   901	
   902	        // Add 3 formula cells all referring to A1.
   903	        wb.put_at(0, 1, 0, Value::Number(0.0)); // stale
   904	        wb.put_formula(0, 1, 0, "A1 + 1");
   905	        wb.put_at(0, 2, 0, Value::Number(0.0));
   906	        wb.put_formula(0, 2, 0, "A1 * 10");
   907	        wb.put_at(0, 3, 0, Value::Number(0.0));

   450	    /// **W5-103 (Phase 4.7.J.4 / Codex W5-102 HIGH-1)** — re-extract
   451	    /// deps for every formula whose `cell_to_formulas` index points at

   454	    /// (its own deps were already (re-)extracted by `on_set_formula`).

   456	    /// Re-extraction means: re-bind the reader's formula text (via the

   458	    /// `name_gen` is stable) and re-run `extract_and_register_deps`. The

   463	    /// Defensive on bind failure: a reader whose formula text no longer

   468	    fn reextract_spill_footprint_readers(
   469	        &mut self,
   470	        anchor_sheet: SheetId,
   471	        anchor_row: RowId,

   481	            let g = match self.graph.as_deref() {
   482	                Some(g) => g,
   483	                None => return, // No graph attached — nothing to re-extract.
   484	            };
   485	            use std::collections::HashSet;
   486	            let mut seen: HashSet<ql_calcgraph::NodeId> = HashSet::new();
   487	            let mut result: Vec<(ql_calcgraph::NodeId, SheetId, RowId, ColId, Arc<str>)> =
   488	                Vec::new();
   489	            for shape in [old_shape, new_shape].into_iter().flatten() {

   501	                    };

   503	                        continue;
   504	                    }

   506	                    // cell_to_formulas has a stale entry — defensive skip.
   507	                    let Some(text) = self.workbook.formula_at(s, r, c).cloned() else {

   531	                sheet: reader_sheet,
   532	                name_gen,
   533	                cell_anchor,

   579	                }

   600	    /// **W5-103 (Phase 4.7.J.2 / 4.7.J.3)** — spill writeback path.
   601	    /// Called from `set_formula` when `eval_at_cell_boundary` returns
   602	    /// `EvalResult::Array`. Performs (per design § 8.1):
   603	    ///
   604	    /// 1. Degenerate check (`rows == 0 || cols == 0` → `#CALC!`).
   605	    /// 2. Bounds check (anchor + shape fits within grid → `#SPILL!`).
   606	    /// 3. Blocking check (any non-anchor target cell occupied → `#SPILL!`).

   609	    ///
   610	    /// Anchor cell ALWAYS gets a formula association (`put_formula`).

   613	    /// Returns `(anchor_value, Option<SpillShape>)`. `Some(shape)` only

   618	    ///
   619	    /// **Tier D1 Step 3.7:** visibility raised to `pub(super)` so

   622	    /// same-module private fn; after the split, sibling-submodule
   623	    /// access requires explicit visibility.
   624	    pub(super) fn write_spill(
   625	        &mut self,
   626	        sheet: SheetId,
   627	        row: RowId,
   628	        col: ColId,
   629	        array: ArrayValue,
   630	        formula_text: Arc<str>,
   631	    ) -> (Value, Option<SpillShape>) {

   633	        // verify follow-up). `set_formula` validates `sheet < sheet_count()`
   634	        // at entry; single-threaded code with no Drop hooks means the
   635	        // sheet cannot disappear mid-function. Per CLAUDE.md "no
   636	        // fallbacks — errors must be visible," check the invariant
   637	        // ONCE at the top of `write_spill` so it fires uniformly even
   638	        // for 1×1 spills (which skip the per-target blocking loop) or
   639	        // bounds/degenerate exits (which would otherwise miss the
   640	        // check). The bound is unused on the degenerate path but
   641	        // present even there so a future code change can't accidentally
   642	        // bypass the assertion.
   643	        let _sheet_invariant = self
   644	            .workbook
   645	            .sheet(sheet)
   646	            .expect("write_spill: sheet validated at set_formula entry");
   647	
   648	        // Degenerate-array origin is a function-eval contract (e.g.
   649	        // FILTER with all-false mask without if_empty). Surface as
   650	        // #CALC!, NOT #SPILL!. Design § 8.1 step c.
   651	        if array.is_degenerate() {
   652	            return self.write_anchor_error(sheet, row, col, ErrorValue::Calc, formula_text);
   653	        }
   654	
   655	        // Bounds check. `array.rows() / cols()` are `>= 1` (degenerate
   656	        // path above caught zero). The last cell sits at
   657	        // `(row + rows - 1, col + cols - 1)` — both must satisfy
   658	        // `<= MAX_ROW / MAX_COLUMN`.
   659	        let last_row_offset = array.rows() - 1;
   660	        let last_col_offset = array.cols() - 1;
   661	        let end_row = row.checked_add(last_row_offset);

   671	        // value (user or computed), no other spill anchor. The clear-old

   687	                    .sheet(sheet)
   688	                    .expect("write_spill: sheet asserted at function entry");

   690	                // include `spill_target_anchor` in the predicate so a
   691	                // cell that is a TARGET of another active spill blocks
   692	                // us even when its materialized value happens to be
   693	                // `Value::Blank` (possible once SEQUENCE/FILTER land

   696	                // letting register_spill panic at a collision later).

   698	                    || self.workbook.spill_target_anchor(sheet, r, c).is_some()
   699	                    || self.workbook.formula_at(sheet, r, c).is_some()

   712	
   713	        // Happy path. Register the spill THEN materialize cells.

     1	//! Sparse per-sheet cell-format overlay (Phase 4.5.D part 3, W5-79).

     5	//!
     6	//! Per design doc § 7.2: most cells have General format, so a per-cell
     7	//! `Option<FormatId>` field would cost 8 bytes × N cells, mostly None.
     8	//! The sparse map keyed by `(RowId, ColId)` costs proportional to the
     9	//! number of cells with actual custom formats — a small fraction in
    10	//! typical workbooks.
    11	//!
    12	//! **V1 design choice (W5-79):** single `HashMap<(RowId, ColId),
    13	//! FormatId>` per sheet. The design doc § 7.2 sketches a chunk-based
    14	//! shape; benchmarking decides if that's worth the structural complexity.
    15	//! For now, a flat map is correct + simple, and the per-sheet shape
    16	//! mirrors how `NameTable` lives on the workbook (a flat map that scales
    17	//! to ~50k entries without trouble).
    18	
    19	use std::collections::HashMap;
    20	
    21	use ql_types::{ColId, RowId};
    22	
    23	use crate::format::FormatId;
    24	
    25	/// Sparse per-sheet `(row, col) → FormatId` overlay.
    26	///
    27	/// Cells absent from the overlay render via `FormatId::GENERAL`.
    28	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
    29	pub struct CellFormatOverlay {
    30	    entries: HashMap<(RowId, ColId), FormatId>,
    31	}
    32	
    33	impl CellFormatOverlay {
    34	    /// Empty overlay.
    35	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    36	        Self::default()
    37	    }
    38	
    39	    /// Read the format-id at `(row, col)`. `None` ≡ render via
    40	    /// `FormatId::GENERAL`.
    41	    pub fn get(&self, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Option<FormatId> {
    42	        self.entries.get(&(row, col)).copied()
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    /// Set the format-id at `(row, col)`. Returns the prior id at that
    46	    /// cell, if any.
    47	    pub fn set(&mut self, row: RowId, col: ColId, id: FormatId) -> Option<FormatId> {
    48	        self.entries.insert((row, col), id)
    49	    }
    50	
    51	    /// Clear the entry at `(row, col)`. Cell falls back to
    52	    /// `FormatId::GENERAL`. Returns the cleared id, if any.
    53	    pub fn clear(&mut self, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Option<FormatId> {
    54	        self.entries.remove(&(row, col))
    55	    }
    56	
    57	    /// Number of entries currently in the overlay.
    58	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
    59	        self.entries.len()
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// True iff no cells carry a custom format.
    63	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
    64	        self.entries.is_empty()
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    /// Wipe the entire overlay.
    68	    pub fn clear_all(&mut self) {
    69	        self.entries.clear();
    70	    }
    71	
    72	    /// Iterate `((row, col), FormatId)` entries. Order is `HashMap`
    73	    /// iteration order; persistence consumers should sort.
    74	    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = ((RowId, ColId), FormatId)> + '_ {
    75	        self.entries.iter().map(|(addr, id)| (*addr, *id))
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    /// Count entries that reference `id`. Used by FormatTable compaction
    79	    /// (Phase 4.10 / GAP-F-07) to decide if an id has zero refs and can
    80	    /// be reclaimed.
    81	    pub fn count_refs(&self, id: FormatId) -> usize {
    82	        self.entries.values().filter(|fid| **fid == id).count()
    83	    }
    84	}
    85	
    86	#[cfg(test)]
    87	mod tests {
    88	    use super::*;
    89	
    90	    #[test]
    91	    fn default_is_empty() {
    92	        let o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
    93	        assert!(o.is_empty());
    94	        assert_eq!(o.len(), 0);
    95	    }
    96	
    97	    #[test]
    98	    fn get_miss_returns_none() {
    99	        let o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   100	        assert_eq!(o.get(0, 0), None);
   101	    }
   102	
   103	    #[test]
   104	    fn set_then_get() {
   105	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   106	        let id = FormatId(14);
   107	        assert_eq!(o.set(5, 3, id), None);
   108	        assert_eq!(o.get(5, 3), Some(id));
   109	    }
   110	
   111	    #[test]
   112	    fn set_overwrites_and_returns_prior() {
   113	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   114	        assert_eq!(o.set(5, 3, FormatId(14)), None);
   115	        assert_eq!(o.set(5, 3, FormatId(15)), Some(FormatId(14)));
   116	        assert_eq!(o.get(5, 3), Some(FormatId(15)));
   117	    }
   118	
   119	    #[test]
   120	    fn clear_removes_entry() {
   121	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   122	        o.set(5, 3, FormatId(14));
   123	        assert_eq!(o.clear(5, 3), Some(FormatId(14)));
   124	        assert_eq!(o.get(5, 3), None);
   125	    }
   126	
   127	    #[test]
   128	    fn clear_missing_returns_none() {
   129	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   130	        assert_eq!(o.clear(999, 999), None);
   131	    }
   132	
   133	    #[test]
   134	    fn clear_all_wipes_overlay() {
   135	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   136	        o.set(0, 0, FormatId(14));
   137	        o.set(1, 1, FormatId(15));
   138	        o.clear_all();
   139	        assert!(o.is_empty());
   140	    }
   141	
   142	    #[test]
   143	    fn count_refs_returns_match_count() {
   144	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   145	        o.set(0, 0, FormatId(14));
   146	        o.set(0, 1, FormatId(14));
   147	        o.set(0, 2, FormatId(15));
   148	        assert_eq!(o.count_refs(FormatId(14)), 2);
   149	        assert_eq!(o.count_refs(FormatId(15)), 1);
   150	        assert_eq!(o.count_refs(FormatId(999)), 0);
   151	    }
   152	
   153	    #[test]
   154	    fn iter_returns_all_entries() {
   155	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   156	        o.set(0, 0, FormatId(14));
   157	        o.set(1, 1, FormatId(15));
   158	        let collected: Vec<_> = o.iter().collect();
   159	        assert_eq!(collected.len(), 2);
   160	    }
   161	
   162	    #[test]
   163	    fn distinct_addresses_isolated() {
   164	        let mut o = CellFormatOverlay::new();
   165	        o.set(0, 0, FormatId(14));
   166	        o.set(0, 1, FormatId(15));
   167	        o.set(1, 0, FormatId(16));
   168	        assert_eq!(o.get(0, 0), Some(FormatId(14)));
   169	        assert_eq!(o.get(0, 1), Some(FormatId(15)));
   170	        assert_eq!(o.get(1, 0), Some(FormatId(16)));
   171	        assert_eq!(o.get(1, 1), None);
   172	    }
   173	}

     1	//! Workbook-level format-string interning table (Phase 4.5.D part 3,
     2	//! W5-79).
     3	//!
     4	//! `FormatTable` reserves Excel built-in format IDs 0-163 and allocates
     5	//! custom IDs from 164 upward. Pre-populates the parser-relevant subset
     6	//! of built-ins (mini-spec § 10) so cells with the most common formats
     7	//! resolve without any registration calls.
     8	//!
     9	//! See:
    10	//! - `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-dates-times-formats.md` § 7.1 for the
    11	//!   design.
    12	//! - `docs/architecture/2026-05-13-format-string-grammar.md` § 10 for
    13	//!   the parser-relevant built-in subset.
    14	
    15	use std::collections::HashMap;
    16	
    17	/// Type-tag wrapper around the u32 format index. Use `FormatId::GENERAL`
    18	/// for the "no custom format" case (built-in id 0).
    19	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, Default, Eq, Hash, Ord, PartialEq, PartialOrd)]
    20	pub struct FormatId(pub u32);
    21	
    22	impl FormatId {
    23	    /// Excel built-in id 0 — "General". Default for cells with no explicit format.
    24	    pub const GENERAL: FormatId = FormatId(0);
    25	}
    26	
    27	/// First custom (non-built-in) format id. IDs 0-163 are reserved for
    28	/// Excel's built-in table per OOXML / mini-spec § 10.
    29	pub const FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID: u32 = 164;
    30	
    31	/// Excel built-in format strings ship in this table at construction time.
    32	/// Per mini-spec § 10, V1 ships the parser-relevant subset; remaining IDs
    33	/// in the 0-163 range can be added lazily when xlsx import (Phase 4.11)
    34	/// surfaces them.
    35	const BUILTIN_FORMATS: &[(u32, &str)] = &[
    36	    (0, "General"),
    37	    (1, "0"),
    38	    (2, "0.00"),
    39	    (3, "#,##0"),
    40	    (4, "#,##0.00"),
    41	    (9, "0%"),
    42	    (10, "0.00%"),
    43	    (11, "0.00E+00"),
    44	    // id 12 (`"# ?/?"`) — V2 fraction, parser refuses; not pre-registered.
    45	    (14, "m/d/yyyy"),
    46	    (15, "d-mmm-yy"),
    47	    (16, "d-mmm"),
    48	    (17, "mmm-yy"),
    49	    (18, "h:mm AM/PM"),
    50	    (19, "h:mm:ss AM/PM"),
    51	    (20, "h:mm"),
    52	    (21, "h:mm:ss"),
    53	    (22, "m/d/yyyy h:mm"),
    54	    (37, "#,##0 ;(#,##0)"),
    55	    // id 38 — V2 color; parser refuses.
    56	    (45, "mm:ss"),
    57	    // id 46 — V2 elapsed; parser refuses.
    58	    (49, "@"),
    59	];
    60	
    61	/// Workbook-level format-string interning + dedup table.
    62	///
    63	/// Cells reference a `FormatId`; the table resolves the id back to a
    64	/// format string the renderer can parse.
    65	#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
    66	pub struct FormatTable {
    67	    /// `id → string`. Sparse (only entries that exist).
    68	    by_id: HashMap<FormatId, String>,
    69	    /// `string → id`. Used by `intern` for dedup.
    70	    by_string: HashMap<String, FormatId>,
    71	    /// Next custom id to hand out. Always >= [`FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID`].
    72	    next_custom_id: u32,
    73	}
    74	
    75	impl Default for FormatTable {
    76	    fn default() -> Self {
    77	        let mut t = Self {
    78	            by_id: HashMap::new(),
    79	            by_string: HashMap::new(),
    80	            next_custom_id: FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID,
    81	        };
    82	        for (id, s) in BUILTIN_FORMATS {
    83	            let fid = FormatId(*id);
    84	            t.by_id.insert(fid, (*s).to_string());
    85	            t.by_string.insert((*s).to_string(), fid);
    86	        }
    87	        t
    88	    }
    89	}
    90	
    91	impl FormatTable {
    92	    /// Fresh table with Excel built-ins (mini-spec § 10 subset) pre-loaded.
    93	    pub fn new() -> Self {
    94	        Self::default()
    95	    }
    96	
    97	    /// Look up the format string for `id`. Returns `None` if the id has
    98	    /// never been registered (which includes most reserved 0-163 ids that
    99	    /// weren't pre-populated by V1 — those land via xlsx import).
   100	    pub fn lookup(&self, id: FormatId) -> Option<&str> {
   101	        self.by_id.get(&id).map(|s| s.as_str())
   102	    }
   103	
   104	    /// Intern `s`. Returns the existing id if `s` is already present;
   105	    /// otherwise allocates a new custom id (≥ [`FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID`])
   106	    /// and stores both directions.
   107	    ///
   108	    /// **Replay determinism:** the allocator is monotonic in insertion
   109	    /// order. As long as the op-log replays `Op::RegisterFormat` events
   110	    /// in the same order they were originally written, custom ids
   111	    /// reconstruct identically.
   112	    pub fn intern(&mut self, s: &str) -> FormatId {
   113	        if let Some(id) = self.by_string.get(s) {
   114	            return *id;
   115	        }
   116	        let id = FormatId(self.next_custom_id);
   117	        self.next_custom_id += 1;
   118	        self.by_id.insert(id, s.to_string());
   119	        self.by_string.insert(s.to_string(), id);
   120	        id
   121	    }
   122	
   123	    /// Register `s` at a specific id (typically an Excel built-in or a
   124	    /// replay path). Returns `Err` if the id is already taken with a
   125	    /// different string — that would break round-trip determinism.
   126	    ///
   127	    /// **Replay use case:** `Op::RegisterFormat { id, string }` calls
   128	    /// this to reproduce the original allocator state.
   129	    pub fn register_at(&mut self, id: FormatId, s: &str) -> Result<(), FormatTableError> {
   130	        if let Some(existing) = self.by_id.get(&id) {
   131	            if existing == s {
   132	                return Ok(());
   133	            }
   134	            return Err(FormatTableError::IdCollision {
   135	                id,
   136	                existing: existing.clone(),
   137	                attempted: s.to_string(),
   138	            });
   139	        }
   140	        if let Some(existing_id) = self.by_string.get(s) {
   141	            if *existing_id == id {
   142	                return Ok(());
   143	            }
   144	            return Err(FormatTableError::StringCollision {
   145	                string: s.to_string(),
   146	                existing_id: *existing_id,
   147	                attempted_id: id,
   148	            });
   149	        }
   150	        self.by_id.insert(id, s.to_string());
   151	        self.by_string.insert(s.to_string(), id);
   152	        if id.0 >= self.next_custom_id {
   153	            self.next_custom_id = id.0 + 1;
   154	        }
   155	        Ok(())
   156	    }
   157	
   158	    /// Total entries — useful for sanity checks, not for hot paths.
   159	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
   160	        self.by_id.len()
   161	    }
   162	
   163	    /// True iff the table has no entries (should only be possible if a
   164	    /// caller clears it; default construction loads built-ins).
   165	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
   166	        self.by_id.is_empty()
   167	    }
   168	
   169	    /// The id `intern` will allocate on its next miss. Exposed for tests
   170	    /// + replay state-check.
   171	    pub fn next_custom_id(&self) -> u32 {
   172	        self.next_custom_id
   173	    }
   174	
   175	    /// Iterate `(id, &str)` in arbitrary order. Persistence + op-log
   176	    /// snapshotting consumers must sort by id when wire-stable order
   177	    /// matters.
   178	    pub fn iter(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (FormatId, &str)> {
   179	        self.by_id.iter().map(|(id, s)| (*id, s.as_str()))
   180	    }
   181	}
   182	
   183	/// Errors from `register_at` collisions.
   184	#[derive(Clone, Debug, Eq, PartialEq)]
   185	#[non_exhaustive]
   186	pub enum FormatTableError {
   187	    /// An id is already taken with a different string.
   188	    IdCollision {
   189	        id: FormatId,
   190	        existing: String,
   191	        attempted: String,
   192	    },
   193	    /// A string is already mapped to a different id.
   194	    StringCollision {
   195	        string: String,
   196	        existing_id: FormatId,
   197	        attempted_id: FormatId,
   198	    },
   199	}
   200	
   201	#[cfg(test)]
   202	mod tests {
   203	    use super::*;
   204	
   205	    #[test]
   206	    fn default_pre_populates_builtins() {
   207	        let t = FormatTable::new();
   208	        assert_eq!(t.lookup(FormatId::GENERAL), Some("General"));
   209	        assert_eq!(t.lookup(FormatId(14)), Some("m/d/yyyy"));
   210	        assert_eq!(t.lookup(FormatId(49)), Some("@"));
   211	    }
   212	
   213	    #[test]
   214	    fn unregistered_builtin_returns_none() {
   215	        // id 12 (fraction) is V2-deferred and NOT pre-populated.
   216	        let t = FormatTable::new();
   217	        assert_eq!(t.lookup(FormatId(12)), None);
   218	    }
   219	
   220	    #[test]
   221	    fn intern_existing_returns_same_id() {
   222	        let mut t = FormatTable::new();
   223	        let id = t.intern("0.00");
   224	        assert_eq!(id, FormatId(2));
   225	        // Re-intern same string returns same id.
   226	        assert_eq!(t.intern("0.00"), id);
   227	    }
   228	
   229	    #[test]
   230	    fn intern_new_allocates_custom_id() {
   231	        let mut t = FormatTable::new();
   232	        let id = t.intern("\"€\" #,##0.00");
   233	        assert_eq!(id, FormatId(FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID));
   234	        // Stored both directions.
   235	        assert_eq!(t.lookup(id), Some("\"€\" #,##0.00"));
   236	        assert_eq!(t.intern("\"€\" #,##0.00"), id);
   237	    }
   238	
   239	    #[test]
   240	    fn intern_two_distinct_strings_get_sequential_ids() {
   241	        let mut t = FormatTable::new();
   242	        let a = t.intern("CUSTOM_A");
   243	        let b = t.intern("CUSTOM_B");
   244	        assert_eq!(b.0, a.0 + 1);
   245	    }
   246	
   247	    #[test]
   248	    fn next_custom_id_advances_on_intern() {
   249	        let mut t = FormatTable::new();
   250	        assert_eq!(t.next_custom_id(), FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID);
   251	        let _ = t.intern("CUSTOM");
   252	        assert_eq!(t.next_custom_id(), FIRST_CUSTOM_FORMAT_ID + 1);
   253	    }
   254	
   255	    #[test]
   256	    fn register_at_replays_known_id() {
   257	        let mut t = FormatTable::new();
   258	        t.register_at(FormatId(200), "REPLAY").unwrap();
   259	        assert_eq!(t.lookup(FormatId(200)), Some("REPLAY"));
   260	        // Next intern should land at 201 (past the manual registration).

   960	    /// Phase 2B.5 (2026-05-12): clear a cell's formula association through
   961	    /// the runtime, emitting `Op::ClearFormula` into the attached op log (if
   962	    /// any). Idempotent: clearing a cell with no formula is a no-op for the
   963	    /// workbook AND for the op log (no entry emitted) — avoiding spurious
   964	    /// "removed nothing" entries.
   965	    ///
   966	    /// Note: `set_value` already emits `ClearFormula` automatically when it
   967	    /// overwrites a formula cell. This method is for callers that want to
   968	    /// explicitly strip a formula without changing the cell's value.
   969	    ///
   970	    /// Direct callers of `Workbook::clear_formula` bypass the op log
   971	    /// silently. See GAP-O-03 in `docs/known-gaps.md`.
   972	    pub fn clear_formula(
   973	        &mut self,
   974	        sheet: SheetId,
   975	        row: RowId,
   976	        col: ColId,
   977	    ) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   978	        validate_cell(self.workbook, sheet, row, col)?;
   979	        let had_formula = self.workbook.formula_at(sheet, row, col).is_some();
   980	        if !had_formula {
   981	            // No-op: nothing to record, nothing to mutate.
   982	            return Ok(());
   983	        }
   984	
   985	        // **W5-103 megaudit MEDIUM-4 closure / design § 10.3:** if this
   986	        // cell anchors a spill, dissolve the spill BEFORE clearing the
   987	        // formula. Without this, `SpillAnchorTable` keeps dangling
   988	        // anchor + target entries and the target cells' computed
   989	        // overlays survive as orphan values — Sonnet megaudit caught
   990	        // this gap.
   991	        //
   992	        // **W5-103 megaudit MEDIUM-4 follow-up — op-log atomicity:**
   993	        // mirror the HIGH-3 fix for set_formula. clear_spill_at MUST
   994	        // run AFTER op-log append, otherwise a failing append leaves
   995	        // the spill dissolved with no recorded op (workbook/log
   996	        // divergence). We capture the dissolved_shape via a read-only
   997	        // `spill_anchor_at` lookup BEFORE append, and only perform
   998	        // the actual `clear_spill_at` mutation AFTER the op-log
   999	        // commits successfully.
  1000	        //
  1001	        // Re-extraction of readers indexed under the dissolved
  1002	        // footprint shipped in W5-108 (Phase 4.7.O) via the
  1003	        // `reextract_spill_footprint_readers` call after the
  1004	        // `on_set_value` pass below.
  1005	        //
  1006	        // **Value preservation semantic for spill anchors:** for a
  1007	        // scalar formula, clear_formula moves the computed value to
  1008	        // the user lane (Excel canon: "convert formula to literal").
  1009	        // For a spill anchor, the anchor's computed value came from
  1010	        // the spilled array, not a scalar result — preserving it
  1011	        // would mis-model the spill semantic. We detect spill-anchor
  1012	        // case here so the op-log emits ONLY ClearFormula (no spurious
  1013	        // PutValue with the array.at(0,0) value).
  1014	        let dissolved_shape = self.workbook.spill_anchor_at(sheet, row, col).copied();

   109	    pub(crate) fn estimate_storage_size(&self, container_type: ContainerType) -> usize {
   110	        match self {
   111	            InnerListOp::Insert { slice, .. } => match container_type {
   112	                ContainerType::MovableList | ContainerType::List => 4 * slice.atom_len(),
   113	                ContainerType::Text => slice.atom_len(),
   114	                _ => unreachable!(),
   115	            },
   116	            InnerListOp::InsertText { slice, .. } => slice.len(),
   117	            InnerListOp::Delete(..) => 8,
   118	            InnerListOp::Move { .. } => 8,

   120	            InnerListOp::StyleStart { .. } => 10,
   121	            InnerListOp::StyleEnd => 1,
   122	        }
   123	    }

   189	impl Mergable for DeleteSpanWithId {
   190	    /// If two spans are mergeable, their ids should be continuous.
   191	    /// LHS's end id should be equal to RHS's start id.
   192	    /// But their spans may be in a reversed order.
   193	    fn is_mergable(&self, rhs: &Self, _conf: &()) -> bool
   194	    where
   195	        Self: Sized,
   196	    {
   197	        let this = self.span;
   198	        let other = rhs.span;
   199	        // merge continuous deletions:
   200	        // note that the previous deletions will affect the position of the later deletions
   201	        match (self.span.bidirectional(), rhs.span.bidirectional()) {
   202	            (true, true) => {
   203	                (this.pos == other.pos && self.id_start.inc(1) == rhs.id_start)
   204	                    || (this.pos == other.pos + 1 && self.id_start == rhs.id_start.inc(1))
   205	            }
   206	            (true, false) => {
   207	                if this.pos == other.prev_pos() {
   208	                    if other.signed_len > 0 {
   209	                        self.id_start.inc(1) == rhs.id_start
   210	                    } else {
   211	                        self.id_start == rhs.id_end()
   212	                    }
   213	                } else {
   214	                    false
   215	                }
   216	            }
   217	            (false, true) => {
   218	                if this.next_pos() == other.pos {
   219	                    if this.signed_len > 0 {
   220	                        self.id_end() == rhs.id_start
   221	                    } else {
   222	                        self.id_start == rhs.id_start.inc(1)
   223	                    }
   224	                } else {
   225	                    false
   226	                }
   227	            }
   228	            (false, false) => {
   229	                if this.next_pos() == other.pos && this.direction() == other.direction() {
   230	                    if self.span.signed_len > 0 {
   231	                        self.id_end() == rhs.id_start
   232	                    } else {
   233	                        self.id_start == rhs.id_end()
   234	                    }
   235	                } else {
   236	                    false
   237	                }
   238	            }
   239	        }
   240	    }
   241	
   242	    fn merge(&mut self, rhs: &Self, _conf: &())
   243	    where
   244	        Self: Sized,
   245	    {
   246	        self.id_start.counter = rhs.id_start.counter.min(self.id_start.counter);
   247	        self.span.merge(&rhs.span, &())
   248	    }
   249	}

    96	    #[inline(always)]
    97	    fn calc_cache_internal(
    98	        cache: &mut Self::Cache,
    99	        caches: &[generic_btree::Child<Self>],
   100	    ) -> Self::CacheDiff {
   101	        let mut new_cache = 0;
   102	        for child in caches {
   103	            new_cache += child.cache;
   104	        }
   105	
   106	        let diff = new_cache - *cache;
   107	        *cache = new_cache;
   108	        diff
   109	    }

   131	    fn sub_cache(cache_lhs: &Self::Cache, cache_rhs: &Self::Cache) -> Self::CacheDiff {

   142	impl ListState {
   143	    pub fn new(idx: ContainerIdx) -> Self {
   144	        let tree = BTree::new();
   145	        Self {
   146	            idx,
   147	            list: tree,
   148	            child_container_to_leaf: Default::default(),
   149	        }
   150	    }
   151	
   152	    pub fn contains_child_container(&self, id: &ContainerID) -> bool {
   153	        let Some(&leaf) = self.child_container_to_leaf.get(id) else {
   154	            return false;
   155	        };
   156	
   157	        self.list.get_elem(leaf).is_some()
   158	    }
   159	
   160	    pub fn get_child_container_index(&self, id: &ContainerID) -> Option<usize> {
   161	        let leaf = *self.child_container_to_leaf.get(id)?;
   162	        self.list.get_elem(leaf)?;
   163	        let mut index = 0;
   164	        self.list
   165	            .visit_previous_caches(Cursor { leaf, offset: 0 }, |cache| match cache {
   166	                generic_btree::PreviousCache::NodeCache(cache) => {
   167	                    index += *cache;
   168	                }
   169	                generic_btree::PreviousCache::PrevSiblingElem(..) => {
   170	                    index += 1;
   171	                }
   172	                generic_btree::PreviousCache::ThisElemAndOffset { .. } => {}
   173	            });
   174	
   175	        Some(index as usize)
   176	    }
   177	
   178	    pub fn insert(&mut self, index: usize, value: LoroValue, id: IdFull) {
   179	        if index > self.len() {
   180	            panic!("Index {index} out of range. The length is {}", self.len());
   181	        }
   182	
   183	        if self.list.is_empty() {
   184	            let idx = self.list.push(Elem {
   185	                v: value.clone(),
   186	                id,
   187	            });
   188	
   189	            if value.is_container() {
   190	                self.child_container_to_leaf
   191	                    .insert(value.into_container().unwrap(), idx.leaf);
   192	            }
   193	            return;
   194	        }
   195	
   196	        let (leaf, data) = self.list.insert::<LengthFinder>(
   197	            &index,
   198	            Elem {
   199	                v: value.clone(),
   200	                id,
   201	            },
   202	        );
   203	
   204	        if value.is_container() {
   205	            self.child_container_to_leaf
   206	                .insert(value.into_container().unwrap(), leaf.leaf);
   207	        }
   208	
   209	        assert!(data.arr.is_empty());
   210	    }
   211	
   212	    pub fn push(&mut self, value: LoroValue, id: IdFull) {
   213	        if self.list.is_empty() {
   214	            let idx = self.list.push(Elem {
   215	                v: value.clone(),
   216	                id,
   217	            });
   218	
   219	            if value.is_container() {
   220	                self.child_container_to_leaf
   221	                    .insert(value.into_container().unwrap(), idx.leaf);
   222	            }
   223	            return;
   224	        }
   225	
   226	        let leaf = self.list.push(Elem {
   227	            v: value.clone(),
   228	            id,
   229	        });
   230	
   231	        if value.is_container() {
   232	            self.child_container_to_leaf
   233	                .insert(value.into_container().unwrap(), leaf.leaf);
   234	        }
   235	    }
   236	
   237	    pub fn delete(&mut self, index: usize) -> LoroValue {
   238	        let leaf = self.list.query::<LengthFinder>(&index);
   239	        let leaf = self.list.remove_leaf(leaf.unwrap().cursor).unwrap();
   240	        if leaf.v.is_container() {
   241	            self.child_container_to_leaf
   242	                .remove(leaf.v.as_container().unwrap());
   243	        }
   244	        leaf.v
   245	    }
   246	
   247	    pub fn delete_range(
   248	        &mut self,
   249	        range: impl RangeBounds<usize>,
   250	        mut notify_deletion: Option<&mut Vec<ContainerID>>,
   251	    ) {
   252	        let start: usize = match range.start_bound() {
   253	            std::ops::Bound::Included(x) => *x,
   254	            std::ops::Bound::Excluded(x) => *x + 1,
   255	            std::ops::Bound::Unbounded => 0,
   256	        };
   257	        let end: usize = match range.end_bound() {
   258	            std::ops::Bound::Included(x) => *x + 1,
   259	            std::ops::Bound::Excluded(x) => *x,
   260	            std::ops::Bound::Unbounded => self.len(),
   261	        };
   262	        if end - start == 1 {
   263	            if let LoroValue::Container(c) = self.delete(start) {
   264	                if let Some(notify_deletion) = &mut notify_deletion {
   265	                    notify_deletion.push(c);
   266	                }
   267	            }
   268	            return;
   269	        }
   270	
   271	        let list = &mut self.list;
   272	        let q = start..end;
   273	        let start1 = list.query::<LengthFinder>(&q.start);
   274	        let end1 = list.query::<LengthFinder>(&q.end);
   275	        for v in iter::Drain::new(list, start1, end1) {
   276	            if v.v.is_container() {
   277	                self.child_container_to_leaf
   278	                    .remove(v.v.as_container().unwrap());
   279	                if let Some(notify_deletion) = &mut notify_deletion {
   280	                    notify_deletion.push(v.v.into_container().unwrap());
   281	                }
   282	            }
   283	        }
   284	    }

   286	    // PERF: use &[LoroValue]
   287	    // PERF: batch
   288	    pub fn insert_batch(&mut self, index: usize, values: Vec<LoroValue>, start_id: IdFull) {
   289	        let mut id = start_id;
   290	        for (i, value) in values.into_iter().enumerate() {
   291	            self.insert(index + i, value, id);
   292	            id = id.inc(1);
   293	        }
   294	    }

   363	    fn apply_diff_and_convert(
   364	        &mut self,
   365	        diff: InternalDiff,
   366	        DiffApplyContext { doc, .. }: DiffApplyContext,
   367	    ) -> Diff {
   368	        let InternalDiff::ListRaw(delta) = diff else {
   369	            unreachable!()
   370	        };
   371	        let mut ans: ListDiff = ListDiff::default();
   372	        let mut index = 0;
   373	        let doc = &doc.upgrade().unwrap();
   374	        for span in delta.iter() {
   375	            match span {
   376	                crate::delta::DeltaItem::Retain { retain: len, .. } => {
   377	                    index += len;
   378	                    ans.push_retain(*len, Default::default());
   379	                }
   380	                crate::delta::DeltaItem::Insert { insert: value, .. } => {
   381	                    let mut arr = Vec::new();
   382	                    match &value.values {
   383	                        either::Either::Left(range) => {
   384	                            for i in range.to_range() {
   385	                                let value = doc.arena.get_value(i).unwrap();
   386	                                arr.push(value);
   387	                            }
   388	                        }
   389	                        either::Either::Right(v) => arr.push(v.clone()),
   390	                    }
   391	                    for arr in ArrayVec::from_many(
   392	                        arr.iter()
   393	                            .map(|v| ValueOrHandler::from_value(v.clone(), doc)),
   394	                    ) {
   395	                        ans.push_insert(arr, Default::default());
   396	                    }
   397	                    let len = arr.len();
   398	                    self.insert_batch(index, arr, value.id);
   399	                    index += len;
   400	                }
   401	                crate::delta::DeltaItem::Delete { delete: len, .. } => {
   402	                    self.delete_range(index..index + len, None);
   403	                    ans.push_delete(*len);
   404	                }
   405	            }
   406	        }
   407	
   408	        Diff::List(ans)
   409	    }
   410	
   411	    fn validate_diff(&self, diff: &InternalDiff) -> LoroResult<()> {
   412	        let InternalDiff::ListRaw(delta) = diff else {
   413	            unreachable!()
   414	        };
   415	
   416	        let mut cursor = 0usize;
   417	        let mut projected = self.len();
   418	        for span in delta.iter() {
   419	            match span {
   420	                crate::delta::DeltaItem::Retain { retain: len, .. } => {
   421	                    cursor += len;
   422	                    if cursor > projected {
   423	                        return Err(LoroError::internal(format!(
   424	                            "list diff retains {cursor} items but state only has {projected}",
   425	                        )));
   426	                    }
   427	                }
   428	                crate::delta::DeltaItem::Insert { insert: value, .. } => {
   429	                    let len = match &value.values {
   430	                        either::Either::Left(range) => range.to_range().len(),
   431	                        either::Either::Right(_) => 1,
   432	                    };
   433	                    if cursor > projected {
   434	                        return Err(LoroError::internal(format!(
   435	                            "list diff inserts at {cursor} but state only has {projected}",
   436	                        )));
   437	                    }
   438	                    cursor += len;
   439	                    projected += len;
   440	                }
   441	                crate::delta::DeltaItem::Delete { delete: len, .. } => {
   442	                    if cursor + len > projected {
   443	                        return Err(LoroError::internal(format!(
   444	                            "list diff deletes {len} at {cursor} but state only has {projected}",
   445	                        )));
   446	                    }
   447	                    projected -= len;
   448	                }
   449	            }
   450	        }
   451	
   452	        Ok(())
   453	    }
   454	
   455	    fn apply_diff(
   456	        &mut self,
   457	        diff: InternalDiff,
   458	        DiffApplyContext { doc, .. }: DiffApplyContext,
   459	    ) -> LoroResult<()> {
   460	        let doc = &doc.upgrade().unwrap();
   461	        match diff {
   462	            InternalDiff::ListRaw(delta) => {
   463	                let mut index = 0;
   464	                for span in delta.iter() {
   465	                    match span {
   466	                        crate::delta::DeltaItem::Retain { retain: len, .. } => {
   467	                            index += len;
   468	                        }
   469	                        crate::delta::DeltaItem::Insert { insert: value, .. } => {
   470	                            let mut arr = Vec::new();
   471	                            match &value.values {
   472	                                either::Either::Left(range) => {
   473	                                    for i in range.to_range() {
   474	                                        let value = doc.arena.get_value(i).unwrap();
   475	                                        arr.push(value);
   476	                                    }
   477	                                }
   478	                                either::Either::Right(v) => arr.push(v.clone()),
   479	                            }
   480	
   481	                            let len = arr.len();
   482	                            self.insert_batch(index, arr, value.id);
   483	                            index += len;
   484	                        }
   485	                        crate::delta::DeltaItem::Delete { delete: len, .. } => {
   486	                            self.delete_range(index..index + len, None);
   487	                        }
   488	                    }
   489	                }
   490	            }
   491	            _ => unreachable!(),
   492	        }
   493	        Ok(())
   494	    }
   495	
   496	    fn apply_local_op(&mut self, op: &RawOp, _: &Op) -> LoroResult<ApplyLocalOpReturn> {
   497	        let mut ans: ApplyLocalOpReturn = Default::default();
   498	        match &op.content {
   499	            RawOpContent::List(list) => match list {
   500	                ListOp::Insert { slice, pos } => match slice {
   501	                    ListSlice::RawData(list) => match list {
   502	                        std::borrow::Cow::Borrowed(list) => {
   503	                            self.insert_batch(*pos, list.to_vec(), op.id_full());
   504	                        }
   505	                        std::borrow::Cow::Owned(list) => {
   506	                            self.insert_batch(*pos, list.clone(), op.id_full());
   507	                        }
   508	                    },
   509	                    _ => unreachable!(),
   510	                },
   511	                ListOp::Delete(del) => {
   512	                    self.delete_range(del.span.to_urange(), Some(&mut ans.deleted_containers));
   513	                }
   514	                ListOp::Move { .. } => {
   515	                    unreachable!()
   516	                }
   517	                ListOp::StyleStart { .. } => unreachable!(),
   518	                ListOp::StyleEnd { .. } => unreachable!(),
   519	                ListOp::Set { .. } => {
   520	                    unreachable!()
   521	                }
   522	            },
   523	            _ => unreachable!(),
   524	        }
   525	        Ok(ans)
   526	    }

   536	        ))

   567	mod snapshot {
   568	    use std::io::Read;

   574	
   575	    use super::*;
   576	    #[columnar(vec, ser, de, iterable)]
   577	    #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
   578	    struct EncodedListId {
   579	        #[columnar(strategy = "DeltaRle")]
   580	        peer_idx: usize,
   581	        #[columnar(strategy = "DeltaRle")]
   582	        counter: i32,
   583	        #[columnar(strategy = "DeltaRle")]
   584	        lamport_sub_counter: i32,
   585	    }
   586	
   587	    #[columnar(ser, de)]
   588	    struct EncodedListIds {
   589	        #[columnar(class = "vec", iter = "EncodedListId")]
   590	        ids: Vec<EncodedListId>,
   591	    }
   592	
   593	    impl FastStateSnapshot for ListState {
   594	        /// Encodes the ListState snapshot in a compact binary format:
   595	        /// 1. Encodes the list values using postcard serialization
   596	        /// 2. Encodes a table of unique peer IDs
   597	        /// 3. For each element, encodes its ID as:
   598	        ///    - Index of the peer ID in the table (LEB128)
   599	        ///    - Counter (LEB128)
   600	        ///    - Lamport timestamp (LEB128)
   601	        fn encode_snapshot_fast<W: Write>(&mut self, mut w: W) {
   602	            let value = self.get_value().into_list().unwrap();
   603	            postcard::to_io(&*value, &mut w).unwrap();
   604	            let mut peers: ValueRegister<PeerID> = ValueRegister::new();
   605	            let mut ids = Vec::with_capacity(self.len());
   606	            for elem in self.iter_with_id() {
   607	                let id = elem.id;
   608	                let peer_idx = peers.register(&id.peer);
   609	                ids.push(EncodedListId {
   610	                    peer_idx,
   611	                    counter: id.counter,
   612	                    lamport_sub_counter: (id.lamport as i32 - id.counter),
   613	                });
   614	            }
   615	
   616	            let peers = peers.unwrap_vec();
   617	            leb128::write::unsigned(&mut w, peers.len() as u64).unwrap();
   618	            for peer in peers {
   619	                w.write_all(&peer.to_le_bytes()).unwrap();
   620	            }

   583	impl DiffCalculatorTrait for ListDiffCalculator {
   584	    fn start_tracking(&mut self, _oplog: &OpLog, vv: &crate::VersionVector, _mode: DiffMode) {
   585	        if !vv.includes_vv(&self.start_vv) || !self.tracker.all_vv().includes_vv(vv) {
   586	            *self.tracker = RichtextTracker::new_with_unknown();
   587	            self.start_vv = vv.clone();
   588	        }
   589	
   590	        self.tracker.checkout(vv);
   591	    }
   592	
   593	    fn apply_change(
   594	        &mut self,
   595	        _oplog: &OpLog,
   596	        op: crate::op::RichOp,
   597	        vv: Option<&crate::VersionVector>,
   598	    ) {
   599	        if let Some(vv) = vv {
   600	            self.tracker.checkout(vv);
   601	        }
   602	
   603	        match &op.op().content {
   604	            crate::op::InnerContent::List(l) => match l {
   605	                InnerListOp::Insert { slice, pos } => {
   606	                    self.tracker.insert(
   607	                        op.id_full(),
   608	                        *pos,
   609	                        RichtextChunk::new_text(slice.0.clone()),
   610	                    );
   611	                }
   612	                InnerListOp::Delete(del) => {
   613	                    self.tracker.delete(
   614	                        op.id_start(),
   615	                        del.id_start,
   616	                        del.start() as usize,
   617	                        del.atom_len(),
   618	                        del.is_reversed(),
   619	                    );
   620	                }
   621	                _ => unreachable!(),
   622	            },
   623	            _ => unreachable!(),
   624	        }
   625	    }
   626	
   627	    fn finish_this_round(&mut self) {}
   628	
   629	    fn calculate_diff(
   630	        &mut self,
   631	        idx: ContainerIdx,
   632	        oplog: &OpLog,
   633	        info: DiffCalcVersionInfo,
   634	        mut on_new_container: impl FnMut(&ContainerID),
   635	    ) -> (InternalDiff, DiffMode) {
   636	        let mut delta = Delta::new();
   637	        for item in self.tracker.diff(info.from_vv, info.to_vv) {
   638	            match item {
   639	                CrdtRopeDelta::Retain(len) => {
   640	                    delta = delta.retain(len);
   641	                }
   642	                CrdtRopeDelta::Insert {
   643	                    chunk: value,
   644	                    id,
   645	                    lamport,
   646	                } => match value.value() {
   647	                    RichtextChunkValue::Text(range) => {
   648	                        for i in range.clone() {
   649	                            let v = oplog.arena.get_value(i as usize);
   650	                            if let Some(LoroValue::Container(c)) = &v {
   651	                                on_new_container(c);
   652	                            }
   653	                        }
   654	                        delta = delta.insert(SliceWithId {
   655	                            values: Either::Left(SliceRange(range)),
   656	                            id: IdFull::new(id.peer, id.counter, lamport.unwrap()),
   657	                            elem_id: None,
   658	                        });
   659	                    }
   660	                    RichtextChunkValue::StyleAnchor { .. } => unreachable!(),
   661	                    RichtextChunkValue::Unknown(len) => {
   662	                        delta = handle_unknown(idx, id, oplog, len, &mut on_new_container, delta);
   663	                    }
   664	                    RichtextChunkValue::MoveAnchor => {
   665	                        delta = handle_unknown(idx, id, oplog, 1, &mut on_new_container, delta);
   666	                    }
   667	                },
   668	                CrdtRopeDelta::Delete(len) => {
   669	                    delta = delta.delete(len);
   670	                }
   671	            }
   672	        }
   673	
   674	        /// Handle span with unknown content when calculating diff
   675	        ///
   676	        /// We can lookup the content of the span by the id in the oplog
   677	        fn handle_unknown(
   678	            idx: ContainerIdx,
   679	            mut id: ID,
   680	            oplog: &OpLog,
   681	            len: u32,
   682	            on_new_container: &mut dyn FnMut(&ContainerID),
   683	            mut delta: Delta<SliceWithId>,
   684	        ) -> Delta<SliceWithId> {
   685	            // assert not unknown id
   686	            assert_ne!(id.peer, PeerID::MAX);
   687	            let mut acc_len = 0;
   688	            let end = id.counter + len as Counter;
   689	            let shallow_root = oplog.shallow_since_vv().get(&id.peer).copied().unwrap_or(0);
   690	            if id.counter < shallow_root {
   691	                // need to find the content between id.counter ~ target_end in gc state
   692	                let target_end = shallow_root.min(end);
   693	                delta = oplog.with_history_cache(|h| {
   694	                    let chunks =
   695	                        h.find_list_chunks_in(idx, IdSpan::new(id.peer, id.counter, target_end));
   696	                    for c in chunks {
   697	                        acc_len += c.length();
   698	                        match &c.values {
   699	                            Either::Left(_) => unreachable!(),
   700	                            Either::Right(r) => {
   701	                                if let LoroValue::Container(c) = r {
   702	                                    on_new_container(c)
   703	                                }
   704	                            }
   705	                        }
   706	                        delta = delta.insert(c);
   707	                    }
   708	
   709	                    delta
   710	                });
   711	                id.counter = shallow_root;
   712	            }
   713	
   714	            if id.counter < end {
   715	                for rich_op in oplog.iter_ops(IdSpan::new(id.peer, id.counter, end)) {
   716	                    acc_len += rich_op.content_len();
   717	                    let op = rich_op.op();
   718	                    let lamport = rich_op.lamport();
   719	
   720	                    if let InnerListOp::Insert { slice, pos: _ } = op.content.as_list().unwrap() {
   721	                        let range = slice.clone();
   722	                        for i in slice.0.clone() {
   723	                            let v = oplog.arena.get_value(i as usize);
   724	                            if let Some(LoroValue::Container(c)) = &v {
   725	                                (on_new_container)(c);
   726	                            }
   727	                        }
   728	
   729	                        delta = delta.insert(SliceWithId {
   730	                            values: Either::Left(range),
   731	                            id: IdFull::new(id.peer, op.counter, lamport),
   732	                            elem_id: None,
   733	                        });
   734	                    } else if let InnerListOp::Move { elem_id, .. } = op.content.as_list().unwrap()
   735	                    {
   736	                        delta = delta.insert(SliceWithId {
   737	                            // We do NOT need an actual value range,
   738	                            // movable list container will only use the id info
   739	                            values: Either::Right(LoroValue::Null),
   740	                            id: IdFull::new(id.peer, op.counter, lamport),
   741	                            elem_id: Some(elem_id.compact()),
   742	                        });
   743	                    }
   744	                }
   745	            }
   746	
   747	            debug_assert_eq!(acc_len, len as usize);
   748	            delta
   749	        }
   750

   232	    ///
   233	    /// - If reversed is true, the deletion will be done in reversed order.
   234	    ///   But the start_id always refers to the first delete op's id.
   235	    /// - If reversed is true, the returned `SplittedLeaves` will be in reversed order.
   236	    pub(super) fn delete(
   237	        &mut self,
   238	        mut start_id: ID,
   239	        pos: usize,
   240	        len: usize,
   241	        reversed: bool,
   242	        notify_deleted_span: &mut dyn FnMut(&FugueSpan),
   243	    ) -> SmallVec<[SplittedLeaves; 1]> {
   244	        if len == 0 {
   245	            return Default::default();
   246	        }
   247	
   248	        if reversed && len > 1 {
   249	            // TODO: PERF: optimize this
   250	            let mut ans = SmallVec::with_capacity(len);
   251	            for i in (0..len).rev() {
   252	                let a = self.delete(
   253	                    start_id.inc(i as i32),
   254	                    pos + i,
   255	                    1,
   256	                    false,
   257	                    notify_deleted_span,
   258	                );
   259	
   260	                ans.extend(a);
   261	            }
   262	
   263	            return ans;
   264	        }
   265	
   266	        let start = self
   267	            .tree
   268	            .query::<ActiveLenQueryPreferRight>(&(pos as i32))
   269	            .unwrap();
   270	        // avoid pointing to the end of the node
   271	        let start = start.cursor;
   272	        let elem = self.tree.get_elem_mut(start.leaf).unwrap();
   273	        if elem.rle_len() >= start.offset + len {
   274	            // tracing::info!("len={} offset={} l={} ", elem.rle_len(), start.offset, len,);
   275	            let (_, splitted) = self.tree.update_leaf(start.leaf, |elem| {
   276	                let (a, b) = elem.update_with_split(start.offset..start.offset + len, |elem| {
   277	                    assert!(elem.is_activated());
   278	                    debug_assert_eq!(len, elem.rle_len());
   279	                    notify_deleted_span(elem);
   280	                    elem.status.delete_times += 1;
   281	                    if elem.real_id.is_none() {
   282	                        elem.real_id = Some(start_id.try_into().unwrap());
   283	                    }
   284	
   285	                    start_id = start_id.inc(elem.rle_len() as i32);
   286	                });
   287	
   288	                (true, a, b)
   289	            });
   290	
   291	            return smallvec![splitted];
   292	        }
   293	
   294	        let end = self
   295	            .tree
   296	            .query::<ActiveLenQueryPreferLeft>(&((pos + len) as i32))
   297	            .unwrap();
   298	        smallvec![self.tree.update(start..end.cursor(), &mut |elem| {
   299	            if elem.is_activated() {
   300	                notify_deleted_span(elem);
   301	                elem.status.delete_times += 1;
   302	                if elem.real_id.is_none() {
   303	                    elem.real_id = Some(start_id.try_into().unwrap());
   304	                }
   305	
   306	                start_id = start_id.inc(elem.rle_len() as i32);
   307	                Some(Cache {
   308	                    len: -(elem.rle_len() as i32),
   309	                    changed_num: 0,
   310	                })
   311	            } else {
   312	                None
   313	            }
   314	        })]
   315	    }
   316	
   317	    #[allow(unused)]
   318	    pub(crate) fn diagnose(&self) {
   319	        println!("crdt_rope number of tree nodes = {}", self.tree.node_len());
   320	    }
   321	
   322	    /// Update the leaf with given `id_span`
   323	    ///
   324	    /// Return the new leaf indexes that are created by splitting the old leaf nodes
   325	    pub(super) fn update(
   326	        &mut self,
   327	        mut updates: Vec<LeafUpdate>,
   328	        on_diff_status: bool,
   329	    ) -> Vec<LeafIndex> {
   330	        updates.sort_by_key(|x| x.leaf);
   331	        let mut tree_update_info = Vec::with_capacity(updates.len());
   332	        for (leaf, group) in &updates.into_iter().group_by(|x| x.leaf) {
   333	            let elem = self.tree.get_elem(leaf).unwrap();
   334	            for u in group {
   335	                debug_assert_eq!(u.id_span.peer, elem.id.peer);
   336	                let start = (u.id_span.ctr_start() - elem.id.counter).max(0);
   337	                let end = (u.id_span.ctr_end() - elem.id.counter).max(0);
   338	                let len = elem.rle_len();
   339	
   340	                tree_update_info.push((leaf, (start as usize).min(len)..(end as usize).min(len), u))
   341	            }
   342	        }
   343	
   344	        self.tree
   345	            .update_leaves_with_arg_in_ranges(tree_update_info, |elem, arg| {
   346	                let status = if on_diff_status {
   347	                    if elem.diff_status.is_none() {
   348	                        elem.diff_status = Some(elem.status);
   349	                    }
   350	                    elem.diff_status.as_mut().unwrap()
   351	                } else {
   352	                    &mut elem.status
   353	                };
   354	
   355	                if let Some(f) = arg.set_future {
   356	                    status.future = f;
   357	                }
   358	
   359	                status.delete_times += arg.delete_times_diff;
   360	            })

   365	            |cache| {
   366	                let drill = cache.changed_num > 0;
   367	                cache.changed_num = 0;
   368	                drill
   369	            },
   370	            |elem| {
   371	                elem.diff_status = None;
   372	            },
   373	        );
   374	    }
   375	
   376	    pub(super) fn get_diff(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = CrdtRopeDelta> + '_ {
   377	        let mut last_pos = 0;
   378	        let mut iter = self
   379	            .tree
   380	            .iter_with_filter(|cache| (cache.changed_num > 0, cache.len));
   381	        let mut next = None;
   382	        std::iter::from_fn(move || {
   383	            if let Some(next) = next.take() {
   384	                return Some(next);
   385	            }
   386	
   387	            #[allow(clippy::while_let_on_iterator)]
   388	            while let Some((index, elem)) = iter.next() {
   389	                // The elements will not be changed by this method.
   390	                // This index is current index of the elem (calculated by `status` field rather than `diff_status` field)
   391	                match elem.diff() {
   392	                    DiffStatus::NotChanged => {}
   393	                    DiffStatus::Created => {
   394	                        let rt = Some(CrdtRopeDelta::Insert {
   395	                            chunk: elem.content,
   396	                            id: elem.real_id.map(|x| x.to_id()).unwrap(),
   397	                            lamport: if elem.id.peer == UNKNOWN_PEER_ID {
   398	                                None
   399	                            } else {
   400	                                Some(elem.id.lamport)
   401	                            },
   402	                        });
   403	                        if index > last_pos {
   404	                            next = rt;
   405	                            let len = index - last_pos;
   406	                            // last pos = index, because the creation has not been applied to the elem
   407	                            last_pos = index;
   408	                            return Some(CrdtRopeDelta::Retain(len as usize));
   409	                        } else {
   410	                            return rt;
   411	                        }
   412	                    }
   413	                    DiffStatus::Deleted => {
   414	                        let rt = Some(CrdtRopeDelta::Delete(elem.rle_len()));
   415	                        if index > last_pos {
   416	                            next = rt;
   417	                            let len = index - last_pos;
   418	                            // last pos = index + len, because the deletion has not been applied to the elem
   419	                            last_pos = index + elem.rle_len() as i32;
   420	                            return Some(CrdtRopeDelta::Retain(len as usize));
   421	                        } else {
   422	                            last_pos = index + elem.rle_len() as i32;
   423	                            return rt;
   424	                        }
   425	                    }
   426	                }
   427	            }
   428	
   429	            None
   430	        })
   431	    }
   432	
   433	    fn cmp_pos(
   434	        &self,
   435	        parent_right: Option<LeafIndex>,
   436	        other_parent_right: Option<LeafIndex>,
   437	    ) -> Ordering {
   438	        match (parent_right, other_parent_right) {
   439	            (Some(a), Some(b)) => self
   440	                .tree
   441	                .compare_pos(Cursor { leaf: a, offset: 0 }, Cursor { leaf: b, offset: 0 }),
   442	            (Some(_), None) => Ordering::Less,
   443	            (None, Some(_)) => Ordering::Greater,
   444	            (None, None) => Ordering::Equal,
   445	        }
   446	    }
   447	}

   460	        /// know the correct lamport for the chunk.

   464	}

    12	/// `Change` is a grouped continuous operations that share the same id, timestamp, commit message.
    13	///
    14	/// - The id of the `Change` is the id of its first op.
    15	/// - The second op's id is `{ peer: change.id.peer, counter: change.id.counter + 1 }`
    16	///
    17	/// The same applies on `Lamport`:
    18	///
    19	/// - The lamport of the `Change` is the lamport of its first op.
    20	/// - The second op's lamport is `change.lamport + 1`
    21	///
    22	/// The length of the `Change` is how many operations it contains
    23	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    24	pub struct ChangeMeta {
    25	    /// Lamport timestamp of the Change
    26	    pub lamport: Lamport,
    27	    /// The first Op id of the Change

    29	    /// [Unix time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unix_time)

    38	}

   241	    pub fn new_by_change(change: &Change<Op>, op: &'a Op) -> Self {
   242	        let diff = op.counter - change.id.counter;
   243	        RichOp {
   244	            op: Cow::Borrowed(op),
   245	            peer: change.id.peer,

   253	    /// we want the overlap part of the op and change[start..end]
   254	    ///
   255	    /// op is contained in the change, but it's not necessary overlap with change[start..end]
   256	    pub fn new_by_slice_on_change(change: &Change<Op>, start: i32, end: i32, op: &'a Op) -> Self {
   257	        debug_assert!(end > start);
   258	        let op_index_in_change = op.counter - change.id.counter;
   259	        let op_slice_start = (start - op_index_in_change).clamp(0, op.atom_len() as i32);
   260	        let op_slice_end = (end - op_index_in_change).clamp(0, op.atom_len() as i32);

   263	            peer: change.id.peer,
   264	            lamport: change.lamport + op_index_in_change as Lamport,
   265	            timestamp: change.timestamp,

   273	        let op_slice_start = (span.start - op.counter).clamp(0, op.atom_len() as i32);
   274	        let op_slice_end = (span.end - op.counter).clamp(0, op.atom_len() as i32);
   275	        if op_slice_start == op_slice_end {
   276	            return None;
   277	        }
   278	        Some(RichOp {
   279	            op: Cow::Borrowed(op),
   280	            peer: change.id.peer,
   281	            lamport: change.lamport + op_index_in_change as Lamport,
   282	            timestamp: change.timestamp,
   283	            start: op_slice_start as usize,

codex
Loro’s public `push` is just `insert(len, value)`, so I’m tracing the internal list tracker rather than relying on the API name. The implementation points to Fugue-style list insertion with peer-id tiebreaking, not a simple “Lamport LWW” rule.

    22	/// The mode of the export.
    23	///
    24	/// Loro CRDT internally consists of two parts: document history and current document state.
    25	/// The export modes offer various options to meet different requirements.
    26	///
    27	/// - CRDT property: Documents maintain consistent states when they receive the same set of updates.
    28	/// - In real-time collaboration, peers typically only need to synchronize updates
    29	///   (operations/history) to achieve consistency.
    30	///
    31	/// ## Update Export
    32	///
    33	/// - Exports only the history part, containing multiple operations.
    34	/// - Suitable for real-time collaboration scenarios where peers only need to synchronize updates.
    35	///
    36	/// ## Snapshot Export
    37	///
    38	/// ### Default Snapshot
    39	///
    40	/// - Includes complete history and current full state.
    41	///
    42	/// ### Shallow Snapshot
    43	///
    44	/// - Contains the complete current state.
    45	/// - Retains partial history starting from a specified version.
    46	///
    47	/// ### State-only Snapshot
    48	///
    49	/// - Exports the state of the target version.
    50	/// - Includes a minimal set of operation history.
    51	#[non_exhaustive]
    52	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    53	pub enum ExportMode<'a> {
    54	    /// It contains the full history and the current state of the document.
    55	    Snapshot,
    56	    /// It contains the history since the `from` version vector.
    57	    Updates { from: Cow<'a, VersionVector> },
    58	    /// This mode exports the history in the specified range.
    59	    UpdatesInRange { spans: Cow<'a, [IdSpan]> },
    60	    /// The shallow snapshot only contains the history since the target frontiers
    61	    ShallowSnapshot(Cow<'a, Frontiers>),
    62	    /// The state only snapshot exports the state of the target version
    63	    /// with a minimal set of history (a few ops).
    64	    ///
    65	    /// It's a shallow snapshot with depth=1 at the target version.
    66	    /// If the target version is None, it will use the latest version as the target version.
    67	    StateOnly(Option<Cow<'a, Frontiers>>),
    68	    /// The snapshot at the specified frontiers. It contains the full history
    69	    /// till the target frontiers and the state at the target frontiers.
    70	    SnapshotAt { version: Cow<'a, Frontiers> },
    71	}
    72

   232	
   233	    /// Enables editing in detached mode, which is disabled by default.
   234	    ///
   235	    /// The doc enter detached mode after calling `detach` or checking out a non-latest version.
   236	    ///
   237	    /// # Important Notes:
   238	    ///
   239	    /// - This mode uses a different PeerID for each checkout.
   240	    /// - Ensure no concurrent operations share the same PeerID if set manually.
   241	    /// - Importing does not affect the document's state or version; changes are
   242	    ///   recorded in the [OpLog] only. Call `checkout` to apply changes.
   243	    ///
   244	    /// # Example
   245	    /// ```
   246	    /// use loro::LoroDoc;
   247	    ///
   248	    /// let doc = LoroDoc::new();
   249	    /// let v0 = doc.state_frontiers();
   250	    /// // Make some edits…
   251	    /// doc.get_text("t").insert(0, "Hello").unwrap();
   252	    /// doc.commit();
   253	    ///
   254	    /// // Travel back and enable detached editing
   255	    /// doc.checkout(&v0).unwrap();
   256	    /// assert!(doc.is_detached());
   257	    /// doc.set_detached_editing(true);
   258	    /// doc.get_text("t").insert(0, "old").unwrap();
   259	    /// // Later, re-attach to see latest again
   260	    /// doc.attach();
   261	    /// ```
   262	    #[inline]
   263	    pub fn set_detached_editing(&self, enable: bool) {
   264	        self.doc.set_detached_editing(enable);
   265	    }
   266	
   267	    /// Whether editing the doc in detached mode is allowed, which is disabled by
   268	    /// default.
   269	    ///
   270	    /// The doc enter detached mode after calling `detach` or checking out a non-latest version.
   271	    ///
   272	    /// # Important Notes:
   273	    ///
   274	    /// - This mode uses a different PeerID for each checkout.
   275	    /// - Ensure no concurrent operations share the same PeerID if set manually.
   276	    /// - Importing does not affect the document's state or version; changes are
   277	    ///   recorded in the [OpLog] only. Call `checkout` to apply changes.
   278	    #[inline]
   279	    pub fn is_detached_editing_enabled(&self) -> bool {
   280	        self.doc.is_detached_editing_enabled()
   281	    }
   282	
   283	    /// Set the interval of mergeable changes, **in seconds**.
   284	    ///
   285	    /// If two continuous local changes are within the interval, they will be merged into one change.
   286	    /// The default value is 1000 seconds.
   287	    ///
   288	    /// By default, we record timestamps in seconds for each change. So if the merge interval is 1, and changes A and B

docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md:26:`CalcgraphSession` is owned **alongside** the workbook (parallel to `OpLog`), not **inside** the workbook. The reason: `ql-storage::Workbook` is the storage primitive — it has no dependency on `ql-formula-syntax` or `ql-calcgraph`. The graph needs to lex/parse formula text to extract dependencies, which would invert the dep direction. By keeping the graph in `ql-exec` (where lex/parse + storage are both available), the dep graph stays acyclic.
docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md:28:Engine Phase 6.1 (`WorkbookSession`) absorbs `Workbook` + `OpLog` + `CalcgraphSession` + `PlanCache` + `FunctionRegistry` into ONE owning struct that the binding crate can wrap cleanly (per GAP-PS-09). Today these are separate to keep refactor scope bounded.
docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md:123:let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_oplog(&mut wb, &reg, &mut oplog);  // no graph
docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md:125:let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::with_oplog_and_graph(&mut wb, &reg, &mut oplog, &mut graph);
docs/architecture/calcgraph-runtime.md:128:Existing callers (the IDE simulation test, the e2e tests, all the unit tests) continue to use `new` or `with_oplog` and are unaffected. The 4-constructor surface is a transitional shape; Phase 6.1 collapses it.
