   760	    /// `clear_spill_at`. Returns `true` if a spill was cleared, `false`
   761	    /// if no anchor was registered at the cell. Friendlier API for the
   762	    /// runtime spill-writeback path (Phase 4.7.J) which calls "clear
   763	    /// before re-eval" regardless of whether a prior spill existed.
   764	    /// Equivalent to `clear_spill_at(anchor).is_ok()` semantically.
   765	    pub fn clear_spill_if_present(&mut self, anchor: (SheetId, RowId, ColId)) -> bool {
   766	        self.clear_spill_at(anchor).is_ok()
   767	    }
   768	
   769	    /// Read by `Address`.
   770	    ///
   771	    /// - **Missing sheet** (sheet id ≥ `sheet_count()`) → `Value::Error(Ref)`
   772	    ///   (Excel canon: `#REF!`).
   773	    /// - **Within-sheet missing row/col** → `Value::Blank` (Excel canon: empty
   774	    ///   cell coerces to 0 / "" / FALSE in arithmetic / text / logical
   775	    ///   contexts).
   776	    ///
   777	    /// Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M2: the prior implementation returned `Blank`
   778	    /// for missing-sheet too, conflating "sheet deleted" with "empty cell."
   779	    /// The Phase 2A.7 H6 fix corrected this at the evaluator layer
   780	    /// (`ql-exec::WorkbookEnv::read_cell`); this commit aligns the storage
   781	    /// layer's public read API. Any host code that bypasses `WorkbookEnv`
   782	    /// (CLI tools, IDE inspectors, tests) now sees the same `#REF!` semantic.
   783	    pub fn read(&self, addr: Address) -> Value {
   784	        match self.sheet(addr.sheet) {
   785	            Some(s) => s.read(addr.row, addr.col),
   786	            None => Value::Error(ErrorValue::Ref),
   787	        }
   788	    }
   789	
   790	    /// Write by `Address`. Panics if `addr.sheet` is out of bounds (callers must `add_sheet`
   791	    /// first; this avoids implicit-sheet-creation silent bugs).
   792	    pub fn put(&mut self, addr: Address, value: Value) {
   793	        // Capture sheet_count before the mut-borrow so the panic path can use it.
   794	        let total = self.sheet_count();
   795	        match self.sheet_mut(addr.sheet) {
   796	            Some(s) => s.put(addr.row, addr.col, value),
   797	            None => panic!(
   798	                "Workbook::put: sheet {} does not exist (have {})",
   799	                addr.sheet, total
   800	            ),
   801	        }
   802	    }
   803	
   804	    /// Convenience: write a literal row,col,value tuple without constructing an Address.
   805	    /// Phase 3.5: this is the USER-edit path — routes to `Sheet::put` →
   806	    /// `ColumnStore::put` which writes the user overlay AND clears any stale computed
   807	    /// entry at the same cell.
   808	    ///
   809	    /// **Phase 2B.5 (2026-05-12) — LOW-LEVEL.** Bypasses the op log AND the
   810	    /// runtime's bind-plan cache; does NOT clear any pre-existing formula
   811	    /// at the cell (use `clear_formula` separately if needed). Product code
   812	    /// should route through `ql_exec::WorkbookRuntime::set_value`, which
   813	    /// records `Op::PutValue` + an optional `Op::ClearFormula`. Direct
   814	    /// `put_at` is for the qbook loader, the op-log replay path, runtime-
   815	    /// internal pass 2 of formula recompute, and tests. GAP-O-03.
   816	    pub fn put_at(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, value: Value) {
   817	        self.put(Address::new(sheet, row, col), value);
   818	    }
   819	
   820	    /// **Phase 3.5 (2026-05-12) — CORR-25.** Write a FORMULA-OUTPUT value at
   821	    /// `(sheet, row, col)`. Routes to the computed-overlay lane (NOT the user lane).
   822	    /// Used by `WorkbookRuntime::set_formula` (with the freshly-evaluated result of
   823	    /// the formula text) and by `recompute_all` / `recompute_dirty` for every
   824	    /// formula cell in their pass.
   825	    ///
   826	    /// Panics if `sheet` is out of bounds — same contract as `put_at`. Does NOT touch
   827	    /// the user overlay; if a cell is transitioning from "user-typed value" to
   828	    /// "formula", the caller (the runtime) MUST first call `clear_user_at` so the
   829	    /// stale user value doesn't mask the new computed value via the read cascade.
   830	    pub fn put_computed_at(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId, value: Value) {
   831	        let total = self.sheet_count();
   832	        match self.sheet_mut(sheet) {
   833	            Some(s) => s.put_computed(row, col, value),
   834	            None => {
   835	                panic!("Workbook::put_computed_at: sheet {sheet} does not exist (have {total})")
   836	            }
   837	        }
   838	    }
   839	
   840	    /// **Phase 3.5.** Drop the computed-overlay entry at `(sheet, row, col)`. No-op if
   841	    /// the cell has no computed entry or the sheet is out of bounds. Called by
   842	    /// `clear_formula` (the formula text is gone → its output is stale).
   843	    pub fn clear_computed_at(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   844	        if let Some(s) = self.sheet_mut(sheet) {
   845	            s.clear_computed(row, col);

   853	    pub fn clear_user_at(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   854	        if let Some(s) = self.sheet_mut(sheet) {

   862	    /// call to mark a cell as literal-only.
   863	    ///
   864	    /// `formula` is the formula body WITHOUT the leading `=` (matching Excel's
   865	    /// canonical AST representation).
   866	    ///
   867	    /// Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 H2: panics if `sheet >= sheet_count()`, `row >
   868	    /// MAX_ROW`, or `col > MAX_COLUMN`. The prior implementation accepted any
   869	    /// (sheet, row, col), making `validate_cell` at the runtime/transaction layer
   870	    /// bypassable: a `formula_cells` entry with an out-of-bounds coord would
   871	    /// later panic deep inside `Sheet::put` during `recompute_all`'s
   872	    /// `put_at`. Matching the storage-layer convention from `Sheet::put` and
   873	    /// `Workbook::put_at` (which also panic on invariant violation), the
   874	    /// in-process programmer-error path stays loud. Trust-boundary callers
   875	    /// (ql-io loader, IDE host) MUST validate before reaching here.
   876	    pub fn put_formula(
   877	        &mut self,
   878	        sheet: SheetId,
   879	        row: RowId,
   880	        col: ColId,
   881	        formula: impl Into<Arc<str>>,
   882	    ) {
   883	        assert!(
   884	            (sheet as usize) < self.sheets.len(),
   885	            "Workbook::put_formula: sheet {sheet} does not exist (have {})",
   886	            self.sheets.len()
   887	        );
   888	        assert!(
   889	            row <= ql_types::MAX_ROW,
   890	            "Workbook::put_formula: row {row} exceeds MAX_ROW {}",
   891	            ql_types::MAX_ROW
   892	        );
   893	        assert!(
   894	            col <= ql_types::MAX_COLUMN,
   895	            "Workbook::put_formula: col {col} exceeds MAX_COLUMN {}",
   896	            ql_types::MAX_COLUMN
   897	        );
   898	        self.formula_cells.insert((sheet, row, col), formula.into());
   899	    }
   900	
   901	    /// Remove any formula association for a cell. Used when a formula cell becomes a
   902	    /// literal (e.g. user types over the formula with a value). Idempotent: removing
   903	    /// a non-existent entry is a no-op.
   904	    ///
   905	    /// Phase 3.5 (CORR-25): ALSO drops any computed-overlay entry at this cell. A
   906	    /// cell without a formula text has no business carrying a formula output; the
   907	    /// invariant the rest of the engine relies on is "computed overlay populated ⇒
   908	    /// formula text present", and `clear_formula` is the only path that breaks the
   909	    /// formula→computed pair, so the storage layer enforces both sides here.
   910	    ///
   911	    /// **Phase 2B.5 (2026-05-12) — LOW-LEVEL.** Bypasses the op log. Use
   912	    /// `ql_exec::WorkbookRuntime::clear_formula` to record `Op::ClearFormula`
   913	    /// in an attached log. GAP-O-03.
   914	    pub fn clear_formula(&mut self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) {
   915	        self.formula_cells.remove(&(sheet, row, col));
   916	        self.clear_computed_at(sheet, row, col);
   917	    }
   918	
   919	    /// Borrow the formula source for a cell, if it has one.
   920	    pub fn formula_at(&self, sheet: SheetId, row: RowId, col: ColId) -> Option<&Arc<str>> {
   921	        self.formula_cells.get(&(sheet, row, col))
   922	    }
   923	
   924	    /// Count of formula cells across the workbook.
   925	    pub fn formula_count(&self) -> usize {
   926	        self.formula_cells.len()
   927	    }
   928	
   929	    /// Iterate every formula cell as `(sheet, row, col, formula)`. Iteration order is
   930	    /// HashMap-arbitrary; callers that need determinism (qbook save) should sort first.
   931	    pub fn iter_formulas(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (SheetId, RowId, ColId, &Arc<str>)> + '_ {
   932	        self.formula_cells
   933	            .iter()
   934	            .map(|((s, r, c), f)| (*s, *r, *c, f))
   935	    }
   936	}

     1	//! `load_workbook_and_recompute` — Phase 2A.4 convenience.
     2	//!
     3	//! `ql_io::load_workbook` rehydrates a workbook from a `.qbook/` directory but
     4	//! leaves formula values as whatever was saved (could be stale if the workbook
     5	//! was saved before its last edit cycle). Callers that want the formula cells
     6	//! to reflect the current state of their dependencies have to:
     7	//!
     8	//! 1. `let mut wb = ql_io::load_workbook(path)?;`
     9	//! 2. `let mut rt = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut wb, &registry);`
    10	//! 3. `let _ = rt.recompute_all();`
    11	//!
    12	//! Phase 2A.4 collapses that to one call. The IDE's "Open file…" path uses
    13	//! it.
    14	//!
    15	//! ## Phase 2B.2 (2026-05-12)
    16	//!
    17	//! Signature simplified from `Result<Workbook, LoadAndRecomputeError>` to
    18	//! `Result<(Workbook, RecomputeResult), QbookError>`. The prior shape had
    19	//! two failure modes (load error vs recompute error) and stuffed the
    20	//! partially-recomputed workbook into the error variant per Phase 2A.6
    21	//! audit M5. With the new [`RecomputeResult`] aggregating per-cell
    22	//! failures instead of short-circuiting, recompute is no longer fallible
    23	//! at the API level — it always returns a result. Load is the only
    24	//! remaining short-circuit, so the outer `Result` only handles that.
    25	//! Callers inspect `RecomputeResult::is_complete()` / `.failures` to see
    26	//! whether the workbook came back fully consistent. Tracked as GAP-R-02
    27	//! in `docs/known-gaps.md`.
    28	
    29	use std::path::Path;
    30	
    31	use ql_functions::FunctionRegistry;
    32	use ql_io::QbookError;
    33	use ql_storage::Workbook;
    34	
    35	use crate::workbook_runtime::{RecomputeResult, WorkbookRuntime};
    36	
    37	/// Load a `.qbook/` directory and immediately recompute every formula cell.
    38	///
    39	/// Returns `(Workbook, RecomputeResult)` on a successful load. The workbook
    40	/// has every formula re-evaluated where possible; cells whose formulas failed
    41	/// structurally keep their pre-load values, and the failure detail is in
    42	/// `RecomputeResult::failures`. Callers wanting "all-or-nothing" semantics
    43	/// can check `recompute.is_complete()` and discard the workbook on
    44	/// partial-state.
    45	///
    46	/// The recompute does the full lex → parse → bind → eval pipeline for each
    47	/// formula. Iteration order is HashMap-arbitrary (same caveat as
    48	/// `WorkbookRuntime::recompute_all`); intra-workbook formula→formula
    49	/// dependencies may evaluate in an order that produces stale intermediate
    50	/// values. Engine Phase 3 calcgraph integration fixes that (see
    51	/// `docs/MASTER-PLAN.md` Phase 3.4; tracked as GAP-R-01).
    52	///
    53	/// Only `QbookError` short-circuits (file missing, schema mismatch, malformed
    54	/// cell, etc.). Recompute failures are aggregated into `RecomputeResult`
    55	/// without losing the workbook.
    56	pub fn load_workbook_and_recompute(
    57	    path: &Path,
    58	    registry: &FunctionRegistry,
    59	) -> Result<(Workbook, RecomputeResult), QbookError> {
    60	    let mut workbook = ql_io::load_workbook(path)?;
    61	    let recompute = {
    62	        let mut runtime = WorkbookRuntime::new(&mut workbook, registry);
    63	        runtime.recompute_all()
    64	    };
    65	    Ok((workbook, recompute))
    66	}
    67	
    68	#[cfg(test)]
    69	mod tests {
    70	    use super::*;
    71	    use ql_functions::default_registry;

    75	
    76	    fn temp_path(name: &str) -> (TempDir, std::path::PathBuf) {
    77	        let dir = TempDir::new().unwrap();
    78	        let path = dir.path().join(name);
    79	        (dir, path)
    80	    }
    81	
    82	    #[test]
    83	    fn load_and_recompute_refreshes_formula_values() {

    89	
    90	        let mut wb = Workbook::new();

  1641	    #[test]

  1652	            // set_formula → on_set_formula
  1653	            rt.set_formula(0, 1, 0, "A1 + 1").unwrap();
  1654	            // set_value over the formula → on_set_value + on_clear_formula
  1655	            rt.set_value(0, 1, 0, Value::Number(99.0)).unwrap();

  2104	    /// Phase 4.7.J.2 — happy path: `={1,2,3}` at A1 spills 1×3 across
  2105	    /// A1, B1, C1. Return value is array.at(0,0) = 1.

   348	// Phase 2A.1 (2026-05-12): NameTable → NameLookup wiring so the binder can resolve
   349	// `Expr::NameRef` against the workbook's name table. Keeps ql-exec's `plan` module
   350	// agnostic to ql-storage (the trait lives there); this impl bridges them in env.rs
   351	// where ql-storage is already imported.
   352	//
   353	// Phase 2A.6 audit H2: use `lookup_ci` so the binder is robust against callers
   354	// who bypass parser-side canonicalization. The parser already uppercases names in
   355	// `Expr::NameRef`, so this is a safety net for hand-constructed ASTs / tests; it
   356	// removes a case-sensitivity footgun without breaking any happy path.
   357	impl NameLookup for NameTable {
   358	    /// Legacy single-table lookup. Ignores `owning_sheet` — used by tests
   359	    /// that don't need the sheet-scoped chain, and by the legacy
   360	    /// `bind_with_names` entry point. Production callers should pass
   361	    /// `&Workbook` (see the blanket impl below) so sheet-scoped names
   362	    /// resolve correctly per Phase 4.6.D § 7.3.
   363	    fn lookup_named_target(
   364	        &self,
   365	        name: &str,
   366	        _owning_sheet: ql_types::SheetId,
   367	    ) -> Option<ResolvedName> {
   368	        named_target_to_resolved(self.lookup_ci(name)?)
   369	    }
   370	}
   371	
   372	/// **W5-92 (Phase 4.6.D):** the production `NameLookup` impl —
   373	/// `&Workbook` does the two-tier sheet-then-workbook chain per
   374	/// design doc § 7.3:
   375	///
   376	/// 1. If `owning_sheet`'s `scoped_names` table has the name, return it.
   377	/// 2. Else, fall back to the workbook-scoped `NameTable`.
   378	///
   379	/// This matches Excel's resolution rule: sheet-scoped names shadow
   380	/// workbook-scoped names with the same identifier when accessed from a
   381	/// formula on that sheet. The production binder call sites pass
   382	/// `&self.workbook` for the `names` argument; legacy callers that
   383	/// passed `self.workbook.names()` get workbook-only behavior (no chain
   384	/// walk) and won't see sheet-scoped names.
   385	impl NameLookup for ql_storage::Workbook {
   386	    fn lookup_named_target(
   387	        &self,
   388	        name: &str,
   389	        owning_sheet: ql_types::SheetId,
   390	    ) -> Option<ResolvedName> {
   391	        // Tier 1: sheet-scoped lookup against the owning sheet.
   392	        if let Some(sheet) = self.sheet(owning_sheet) {
   393	            if let Some(target) = sheet.scoped_names().lookup_ci(name) {
   394	                return named_target_to_resolved(target);
   395	            }
   396	        }
   397	        // Tier 2: workbook-scoped fallback.
   398	        named_target_to_resolved(self.names().lookup_ci(name)?)
   399	    }
   400	}
   401	
   402	/// **W5-90 (Phase 4.6.B):** the production `SheetResolver` impl —
   403	/// `&Workbook` resolves sheet names via `Workbook::sheet_id_by_name`
   404	/// (the canonicalizing case-insensitive lookup added in W5-86).
   405	/// Production callers (`WorkbookRuntime`, `WorkbookTransaction`,
   406	/// `CalcgraphSession`) pass `&self.workbook` as the `&dyn SheetResolver`
   407	/// argument to `bind_with_names_and_sheets`.
   408	impl crate::plan::SheetResolver for ql_storage::Workbook {
   409	    fn resolve_sheet(&self, name: &str) -> Option<ql_types::SheetId> {
   410	        self.sheet_id_by_name(name)
   411	    }
   412	}
   413

   419	impl crate::plan::TableLookup for ql_storage::Workbook {
   420	    fn lookup_table(&self, name: &str) -> Option<&ql_storage::TableMetadata> {
   421	        self.lookup_table(name)
   422	    }
   423	}

     1	//! `ExprPlan` — execution-ready intermediate representation.
     2	//!
     3	//! Sits between `ql_formula_syntax::Expr` (parser output, may carry `Option<SheetId>` for
     4	//! unresolved same-sheet refs) and the kernel dispatch in `scalar.rs` / `simd.rs` (W4-2).
     5	//! `bind()` walks the Expr tree and resolves every `CellAddr.sheet: Option<SheetId>` to a
     6	//! concrete `SheetId` based on the formula's owning sheet.
     7	//!
     8	//! The plan is "execution-ready" but still tree-shaped — it does NOT yet flatten the
     9	//! expression into a SIMD-friendly opcode stream. That's the role of `kernel_simd` in W4-2,
    10	//! which lowers `ExprPlan::Binary { op: Mul, lhs: CellRef, rhs: Number }` into a
    11	//! `multiversion`-dispatched `Float64Array * f64` call.
    12	//!
    13	//! ## Phase 0 scope
    14	//!
    15	//! Subset of `Expr` variants covered:
    16	//! - `Number(f64)` — numeric literal
    17	//! - `Bool(bool)` — boolean literal
    18	//! - `String(Arc<str>)` — text literal (binds 1:1)
    19	//! - `CellRef { sheet, row, col, abs_col, abs_row }` — sheet RESOLVED to concrete SheetId
    20	//! - `Binary { op, lhs, rhs }` — arithmetic + concat + comparison
    21	//! - `Unary { op, operand }` — unary minus / plus / percent
    22	//!
    23	//! Covered through Phase 4.7:
    24	//! - `RangeRef` — single range used inside a Function call (e.g. `SUM(A:A)`)
    25	//!   — bound via the aggregate-context path (Phase 2B.4 / W5-39 evaluator).
    26	//! - `Function { name, args }` — registry dispatch (Phase 0+).
    27	//! - `Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>)` — array literals (W5-99 / Phase 4.7.F).
    28	//! - `Error(ErrorValue)` — error-sigil literals (W5-99 / Phase 4.7.F).
    29	//!
    30	//! Deferred (NOT bound today):
    31	//! - `Spill(Box<Expr>)` — reserved for Excel's `A1#` spill-range-ref
    32	//!   syntax. Originally anticipated for Phase 4.9 but that phase
    33	//!   closed (W5-138 → W5-152) without adding `A1#` parsing; deferred
    34	//!   to a future Phase.
    35	
    36	use std::sync::Arc;
    37	
    38	use ql_formula_syntax::{AxisSpec, Expr, Operator, SheetRef};
    39	use ql_types::{ColId, ErrorValue, Range, RowId, SheetId, MAX_COLUMN, MAX_ROW};
    40	
    41	/// Bound, execution-ready expression. Sheet refs are concrete.
    42	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
    43	pub enum ExprPlan {
    44	    Number(f64),
    45	    Bool(bool),
    46	    String(Arc<str>),
    47	    CellRef {
    48	        sheet: SheetId,
    49	        row: RowId,
    50	        col: ColId,
    51	        abs_col: bool,
    52	        abs_row: bool,
    53	    },
    54	    Binary {
    55	        op: Operator,
    56	        lhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    57	        rhs: Box<ExprPlan>,
    58	    },
    59	    Unary {
    60	        op: Operator,
    61	        operand: Box<ExprPlan>,
    62	    },
    63	    /// Function call. Args are bound recursively. Phase 0 W4-5: name is preserved
    64	    /// verbatim from the AST (Excel-canonical uppercase normalization happens at the
    65	    /// parser level — W4-5 expects already-uppercased names from the binder). The
    66	    /// scalar evaluator looks up the name in a `FunctionRegistry` at evaluation time.
    67	    Function {
    68	        name: Arc<str>,
    69	        args: Vec<ExprPlan>,
    70	    },
    71	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`
    72	    /// in an aggregate-argument position (e.g. the arg of `SUM`, `AVERAGE`,
    73	    /// `COUNT`). Carries the canonical name + the resolved Range. The scalar
    74	    /// evaluator currently rejects this with `Value::Error(ErrorValue::Calc)`
    75	    /// because aggregate-range evaluation is Engine Phase 3.6 work; the bind
    76	    /// shape lands now so the IDE / op-log surfaces are stable when Phase 3.6
    77	    /// flips the eval on.
    78	    ///
    79	    /// In scalar (non-aggregate) positions, the same `NameRef` produces
    80	    /// [`BindError::NamedRangeInScalarContext`] instead of binding to this
    81	    /// variant.
    82	    AggregateNameRef {
    83	        /// Canonical (uppercase) name as it appears in the NameTable.
    84	        name: Arc<str>,
    85	        /// Pre-resolved range payload. Phase 3.6 evaluator reads from this
    86	        /// directly rather than re-looking-up the name.
    87	        range: Range,
    88	    },
    89	
    90	    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** array literal lowered from `Expr::Array`.
    91	    /// Row-major; outer `Vec` is rows, inner `Vec` is cells. Element-arity
    92	    /// is invariant — every row has the same length. The binder enforces
    93	    /// this (`BindError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`) on every public input AND
    94	    /// the parser already enforces it (`ParseError::ArrayRowArityMismatch`);
    95	    /// defense in depth — the binder can't trust the AST because
    96	    /// `Expr::Array` is a public variant that tests / tools can construct
    97	    /// directly with ragged shapes.
    98	    ///
    99	    /// Inner `ExprPlan`s are restricted to the array-cell subset per
   100	    /// design § 3.3: `Number` / `Bool` / `String` / `Error`. Cell-refs,
   101	    /// function calls, and nested arrays are NOT permitted in v1 (already
   102	    /// rejected at parse time via `ParseError::InvalidArrayCellToken`).
   103	    /// The binder validates the same subset as defense in depth.
   104	    ///
   105	    /// Eval-site dispatch: Phase 4.7.G (W5-101). The runtime materializes
   106	    /// an `ArrayValue` from this plan and routes to either the cell-
   107	    /// boundary spill path (cell context) or `#CALC!` (scalar context),
   108	    /// per design § 6.3.
   109	    Array(Vec<Vec<ExprPlan>>),
   110	
   111	    /// **W5-99 (Phase 4.7.F):** error-sigil literal lowered from
   112	    /// `Expr::Error(ev)`. The evaluator returns `Value::Error(ev)` directly;
   113	    /// the lowering is mechanical (no resolution, no context). Used by
   114	    /// `=#REF!` formulas, `IFERROR(..., #N/A)` fallback args, and error
   115	    /// literals inside `Expr::Array` cells.
   116	    Error(ErrorValue),
   117	
   118	    /// **W5-RT-1 (RT-V1-01):** literal range reference (`A1:B3`) lowered
   119	    /// from `Expr::RangeRef` in `BindContext::ReferenceArg`. Carries the
   120	    /// resolved [`Range`] directly, without requiring a name. Used as an
   121	    /// argument to reference-aware functions (ROWS, COLUMNS, ROW with a
   122	    /// range arg, etc.). Distinct from `AggregateNameRef` (which needs a
   123	    /// name) and `StructuredRef` (which needs a table); the only path
   124	    /// that reaches this variant today is `Expr::RangeRef` bound in
   125	    /// `ReferenceArg` context. See design § 5.4.
   126	    RangeRef {
   127	        range: Range,
   128	    },
   129	
   130	    /// **W5-115 (Phase 4.8.F):** structured table reference resolved
   131	    /// against the workbook's `TableTable`. The binder narrows the
   132	    /// `TableSpecSubtree` source AST to a concrete [`Range`]; eval
   133	    /// (Phase 4.8.G) reads the range like an `AggregateNameRef`.
   134	    ///
   135	    /// `is_this_row == true` indicates the source spec was a `[@Col]`
   136	    /// shorthand (or `[[#This Row], [Col]]`): the resolved range
   137	    /// covers the FULL column's data rows, and the eval layer NARROWS
   138	    /// to a single cell using the formula's own row. This keeps the
   139	    /// plan cache cell-INDEPENDENT for these forms (the resolved
   140	    /// range only depends on the table metadata, not the formula
   141	    /// cell).
   142	    ///
   143	    /// `source` is kept for printer round-trip (the printer renders the
   144	    /// canonical `Table[…]` form, not the resolved range), error
   145	    /// messages, and future invalidation hooks.
   146	    StructuredRef {
   147	        /// Canonical (uppercase) table name. Used by the dep extractor
   148	        /// to register the formula in `table_to_formulas` for
   149	        /// `on_table_*` dirty propagation (Phase 4.8.G).
   150	        table_name: Arc<str>,
   151	        /// Original parsed spec subtree (for printer / diagnostics).
   152	        source: Arc<ql_formula_syntax::TableSpecSubtree>,
   153	        /// Resolved range. For column refs: the column's data range.
   154	        /// For `#Headers`: the header row's columns. For `#All`: the
   155	        /// full footprint. For `[@Col]`: the column's FULL data range
   156	        /// (eval narrows to current row).
   157	        resolved: Range,
   158	        /// `true` iff this is a `[@Col]` / `[[#This Row], [Col]]` form
   159	        /// that requires cell-time row narrowing.
   160	        is_this_row: bool,
   161	    },
   162	}

   164	/// Error during binding. Phase 2A.1 added `UnresolvedName`; Phase 2A.6 added
   165	/// `NamedTargetIs{Blank,Error}`; Phase 2A.11 audit M16 switched to
   166	/// `thiserror::Error` for consistency with the rest of the error surface.
   167	/// Display strings are now user-facing (suitable for IDE diagnostics).
   168	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
   169	#[non_exhaustive]
   170	pub enum BindError {
   171	    /// The Expr contains a variant not supported in this build. Currently
   172	    /// reachable from:
   173	    /// - `Expr::RangeRef` outside a Function context (Phase 4+ region binder).
   174	    /// - `Expr::Spill` — Excel's `A1#` syntax (Phase 4.9).
   175	    /// - `NamedTarget::Range` in scalar position (lands on the more
   176	    ///   specific `NamedRangeInScalarContext` variant; this is the
   177	    ///   catchall for variants the binder doesn't otherwise cover).
   178	    ///
   179	    /// Phase 4.7.F: `Expr::Array` and `Expr::Error` are now SUPPORTED;
   180	    /// degenerate-array shapes surface as `EmptyArrayLiteral`, ragged
   181	    /// shapes as `ArrayRowArityMismatch`, non-literal cells as
   182	    /// `ArrayCellNotLiteral`.
   183	    #[error("unsupported expression variant: {0}")]
   184	    UnsupportedVariant(&'static str),
   185	    /// A `NameRef` was used but the name isn't registered in the active `NameTable`.
   186	    /// Phase 2A.1: equivalent to Excel's `#NAME?` but surfaced at bind time rather
   187	    /// than as a runtime Error value, so the IDE can highlight the offending token
   188	    /// before evaluation.
   189	    #[error("unresolved name {0:?}")]
   190	    UnresolvedName(Arc<str>),
   191	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Blank)`. Phase 2A.6
   192	    /// audit M2: previously this was silently coerced to `Text("")`, producing
   193	    /// nonsense in arithmetic contexts. Now surfaced loudly so callers fix the
   194	    /// data binding.
   195	    #[error("named constant {0:?} is blank; cannot be used in this context")]
   196	    NamedTargetIsBlank(Arc<str>),
   197	    /// A `NameRef` resolved to a `NamedTarget::Constant(Value::Error(_))`. Phase 2A.6
   198	    /// audit M3: previously silently mapped to `UnresolvedName` (wrong error class —
   199	    /// the name IS resolved, just to an error value). Carries the underlying
   200	    /// `ErrorValue` so the IDE can echo `#DIV/0!` / `#REF!` etc. accurately.
   201	    // Phase 2A.13 audit cycle-3 M1: `{1}` (Display) instead of `{1:?}` (Debug).
   202	    // `ErrorValue` has a `Display` impl that renders the canonical Excel sigil
   203	    // (`#DIV/0!` etc.); the prior debug formatter leaked Rust enum identifiers
   204	    // ("DivZero") into IDE diagnostics — the same mistake WS-4 was supposed to
   205	    // close everywhere. Note `{0:?}` is kept deliberately on the name field
   206	    // so the Arc<str> is rendered with surrounding quotes for visual clarity
   207	    // ("named constant \"X\" ..." vs the bare-string ambiguous "named constant X").
   208	    #[error("named constant {0:?} holds an error value: {1}")]
   209	    NamedTargetIsError(Arc<str>, ErrorValue),
   210	    /// Phase 2B.4 (2026-05-12): a `NameRef` resolving to a `NamedTarget::Range`

   214	    /// Distinct from the generic `UnsupportedVariant` so callers can render a

   217	    #[error(
   218	        "named range {0:?} cannot be used in this scalar position; \

   220	    )]

codex
**Punch List**

- **H - V1: Loro list merge is not LWW Lamport.**  
  `docs/architecture/crdt-data-model.md:89`, `:99` says concurrent `LoroList::push` merges by Lamport timestamp and is LWW. Source does not support that. `LoroList::push` becomes a list insert at current len (`loro-internal-1.12.0/src/handler.rs:2765`), and concurrent inserts are preserved, not overwritten. The deterministic ordering rule I found is Fugue/origin-based with peer-id tie-breaks for same insertion site (`loro-internal-1.12.0/src/container/richtext/tracker/crdt_rope.rs:163`, `:192`). So: both pushes preserved = yes; receive-order-independent = yes with unique peer IDs; ordering = causal/origin order, peer-id tie-break for same origin, not LWW Lamport. Fix the design wording.

- **Verified - V2: `BatchCommit` is contiguous as one log entry.**  
  `OpLog::append` serializes the whole `Op` to one JSON string and calls `list.push` once (`crates/ql-oplog/src/log.rs:76`). `BatchCommit` nests `Vec<Op>` inside one variant (`crates/ql-oplog/src/op.rs:137`), then replay expands inner ops sequentially (`crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs:495`). A concurrent peer’s op can land before or after the `BatchCommit` list item, but cannot interleave between its nested ops.

- **H - V3: spill semantics are deterministic, but not as described.**  
  Current replay does not evaluate `PutFormula` or materialize spills per op. Replay only stores formula text (`crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs:328`), while `PutValue` writes the user value (`:315`). `recompute_all` later evaluates formulas from the final workbook state (`crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/recompute.rs:143`), and `write_spill` blocks on occupied target cells (`crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/cells.rs:669`). Therefore `PutFormula(A1=SEQUENCE(3))` plus concurrent `PutValue(A2=5)` deterministically yields spill-blocking in both orders if A2 is occupied at recompute time. Fix the design to say spills are derived from final replay state, or change replay to use per-op runtime semantics / persisted spill effects.

- **H - V4: format id collision is real.**  
  `intern_format` predicts `next_custom_id` before logging (`crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/formats.rs:51`). Two peers from the same base can both emit `RegisterFormat { id: N, string: different }`. Replay calls `register_at` (`crates/ql-oplog/src/replay.rs:451`), and `register_at` rejects same id/different string as `IdCollision` (`crates/ql-storage/src/format.rs:129`). Cleanest fix: make custom format ids globally unique, e.g. `Builtin(u32) | Custom { peer_id, counter }` or a widened `u128`/UUID. Wire impact: `RegisterFormat.id`, `SetCellFormat.id`, `FormatId(pub u32)`, and persisted qbook/xlsx mapping need a schema bump or compatibility layer.

- **H - V5: rename-sheet concurrent formula bug confirmed, with different error shape.**  
  Rename only rewrites formulas visible on the producer at rename time (`crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/sheets.rs:168`) and logs those rewrites plus `RenameSheet` in one `BatchCommit` (`:183`). A concurrent formula `=S!A1+1` is not rewritten in either merge order. Binding later resolves names against current sheets (`crates/ql-exec/src/env.rs:402`, `crates/ql-exec/src/plan.rs:1678`) and fails as `BindError::UnknownSheet("S")`, not `UnresolvedName`. `recompute_all` does not map `UnknownSheet` to `#NAME?` (`crates/ql-exec/src/workbook_runtime/recompute.rs:179`), so storage may retain blank/stale value plus a recompute failure. Fix by storing sheet refs canonically by sheet id, or by adding a causality-aware rename repair policy for concurrent formulas.
